# Linux platform exploration findings (sub-phase 3.0)

The evidence ledger sub-phase 3.0 requires. Every row maps one experiment to observed behavior and a doc-alignment verdict, and every row names the client stack that produced it and the scope its conclusion carries. Rows are graded against `docs/phases.md` Phase 3.

Probe scripts and raw captures are development aids kept outside the repository, so the `probe` cell names the probe area and step rather than a committed path. The `observed` cell quotes the capture field values the conclusion rests on.

## How to read a row

| column | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | stable row id (`L<area>-<n>`), cited by phases.md hunks and the contradictions index at the bottom |
| `probe` | the probe area and step that produced the row |
| `stack` | `dbus` = raw D-Bus via Gio, the product-relevant stack; `gi-atspi` = libatspi convenience reads; `tool` = external tools such as xdotool, xprop, xclip. Combinations are joined by " + " |
| `scope` | `api-contract` = product-wide, holds for any conforming AT-SPI client. `app` = specific to the measured application and version. `box` = specific to this environment. Downstream sub-phases read this column to know what generalizes |
| `phases.md expectation` | the sentence or claim in `docs/phases.md` the probe tested, or none |
| `observed` | the capture field values and measurements the verdict rests on |
| `verdict` | `CONFIRMS`, `CONTRADICTS`, `NEW-EDGE`, `DEFERRED` |
| `action` | what the finding obliges. `DEFERRED` rows carry `needs:` and `closure: 3.<n>`, the Phase 3 sub-phase that closes them |

Stack authority: where a `dbus` row and a `gi-atspi` or `tool` row disagree, the `dbus` row wins, because the Rust adapter speaks raw D-Bus.

## Environment

Every row was measured on the machine below, except the rows whose probe cell names the XFCE container, which was measured in the second environment.

| fact | value |
| --- | --- |
| OS | Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS, kernel 7.0 generic, x86_64 |
| Desktop | GNOME Shell 46.0 (mutter 46.2), X11 session; the Wayland rows (L13) were measured in an isolated headless gnome-shell on the same machine |
| Accessibility and bus | AT-SPI core 2.52, dbus 1.14.10 |
| Toolkits | GTK 3.24.41, GTK 4.14.5, Qt 6.11.0 (PyQt6 6.11.0) |
| Tools | xdotool 3.20160805.1, xclip 0.13, wl-clipboard 2.2.1, wmctrl 1.07 |
| Apps | VS Code 1.142.0, Google Chrome 154, snap Firefox 157 |
| XFCE container | Ubuntu 24.04 arm64 container, Xvfb 1440x900, XFCE 4.18 (xfwm4 compositing), AT-SPI core 2.52, GTK 3.24.41 (Mousepad 0.6.1, Thunar 4.18), GTK 4.14.5 (GNOME Text Editor 46.3), Qt 6.6.1 (PyQt6) |

Floor claims for 22.04 / Fedora 38 / GNOME 42 are not exercised here |

The Wayland half is not exercised (L1-17) |
| L1-5 | Presence check on the cold bus (failing direction for a name-owner check) | dbus | api-contract | §3.1 AT-SPI2 Bus Detection: "Check for `org.a11y.Bus` presence on the D-Bus session bus" | cold private session, 7 kept runs plus warm-up: `pre_name_has_owner: false` while `pre_listed_activatable: true`; `no_auto_start_get_address.remote_error: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.NameHasNoOwner`; plain `GetAddress` then succeeds and `post_name_has_owner: true` (`get_address_first_call.ok: true`) | CONTRADICTS | 3.1: detect with an auto-starting `org.a11y.Bus.GetAddress` call, never with `NameHasOwner` or a no-auto-start call; those report a healthy cold session as missing |
| L1-6 | Presence check on the live session (passing direction) | dbus | box | none | `dbus_live_session.org.a11y.Bus_has_owner.value: [true]`, `dbus_live_session.org.a11y.Bus_activatable: true`, `dbus_live_session.a11y_bus_address.scheme: unix`, `dbus_live_session.org.a11y.atspi.Registry_has_owner.value: [true]`, `dbus_live_session.registry_child_count.value: 19` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: on a warm session both checks agree; the divergence in L1-5 appears only before first activation |
| L1-7 | Missing and broken `org.a11y.Bus` service | dbus | api-contract | §3.1: "If the bus is not running, return `PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED`" | no service file (2 runs): `absent_service[*].get_address_default_flags.remote_error: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.ServiceUnknown`, 0.12-0.47 ms, `listed_activatable: false`; service whose Exec is missing (2 runs): `broken_service_exec_missing[*].get_address_default_flags.remote_error: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.Spawn.ExecFailed`, 1.27-1.43 ms, `listed_activatable: true`; no-auto-start variant returns `NameHasNoOwner` in both | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: map ServiceUnknown (package not installed) and Spawn.ExecFailed (installed but cannot start) to PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED with different `platform_detail`; both fail in under 2 ms, so no timeout budget is needed for the absent case |
| L1-8 | Cold activation latency over raw D-Bus (min of 7, warm-up discarded) | dbus | box | none | `cold_latency_ms.get_address_first_call: {min 6.11, median 6.3, max 7.8}` ms, `connected_to_a11y_bus: {6.45, 6.66, 8.17}`, `registry_usable: {11.21, 11.36, 13.17}`, `warm_get_address: {0.13, 0.15, 0.23}` (n=49); `runs_with_usable_registry: 7` of 7 | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: first-command cost of a cold bus is about 11-13 ms to a usable registry; the adapter needs no startup retry loop on this stack, only one auto-starting call per session |
| L1-9 | Empty registry is a success, not a failure | dbus | api-contract | none | fresh bus: `registry.child_count: 0`, `registry.usable: true`, `a11y_bus_names: [org.a11y.atspi.Registry, org.freedesktop.DBus]`, `session_survivors_while_running: [at-spi-bus-laun, at-spi2-registr, dbus-daemon]` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1 / 3.2: report an answered `ChildCount 0` as "bus up, no apps", separate from a read error; do not map 0 children to a bus fault |
| L1-10 | `org.a11y.Status` properties | dbus | box | §3.1: GNOME hint to check `toolkit-accessibility` | live: `dbus_live_session.IsEnabled.value: true`, `dbus_live_session.ScreenReaderEnabled.value: false`, `gsettings["org.gnome.desktop.interface toolkit-accessibility"]: true`; fresh private session reads `status_IsEnabled.value: true`, `status_ScreenReaderEnabled.value: false` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: `IsEnabled` is readable over D-Bus on every desktop, unlike `gsettings`, but it is not a diagnostic: with it false the bus and apps still work (L1-16) |
| L1-11 | GTK4 app starting before the a11y bus exists (bus activatable) | dbus | app | none | `gtk4_app.cold[0]`: `pre_name_has_owner: false`, then `app_triggered_a11y_bus_activation: true`, `app_registration_from_registry_poll.registered: true`, `tree_nodes: 4`, `tree_read_failures: 0`, `leak_check_shared_a11y_bus.app_found_on_shared_a11y_bus: false` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1 / 3.2: a GTK4 app (4.14.5) itself activates the bus, so the adapter need not start it first |
| L1-12 | GTK4 app tree shape with the bus present (warm, passing direction) | dbus | app | none | `gtk4_app.warm[0]`: nodes by depth: `application` ("python3", Accessible+Application) -> `application` ("BusProbe window", Accessible+Action+Component) -> `button` ("Probe button", Action+Component) -> `label` ("Probe button", Accessible+Action+Component+Text); `tree_nodes: 4`, `tree_read_failures: 0`, identical to the cold run | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: GTK4 `ApplicationWindow` reports role name `application` through `GetRoleName`, not `frame` or `window`; the role map must not assume a window role from GTK4. The `label` child repeats the button's name and advertises Action. Revisited by numeric role in L3-2: the GTK4 window is role number 23 (frame) |
| L1-13 | GTK4 app starting before the bus, bus started after (failing direction, 2 independent runs) | dbus | app | none | `gtk4_app.late[0]` and `[1]`: `a11y_bus_present_when_app_ready: false`, `late_bus_name_has_owner: true` (started in about 11 ms), `app_registration_from_registry_poll.registered: false` after 12 s (about 1155 polls), `registry_children_seen: 0`; in both runs `leak_check_shared_a11y_bus.app_found_on_shared_a11y_bus: true` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1 / 3.2: an app that started with no bus never registers on a bus that appears later, so a snapshot cannot see it until the app restarts; in this case the app registered on a different (shared) a11y bus, so the adapter must resolve the bus from `GetAddress` and not assume one bus per login. The mechanism that selected the other bus was not isolated |
| L1-14 | Bus daemon lifecycle on session exit | tool | api-contract | none | every scenario: `cleanup.survivors_after_session_daemon_exit: []`, `cleanup.survivors_after_cleanup: 0` (launcher, registryd and a11y dbus-daemon all exit with their session bus) | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: nothing to clean up after an activated bus; tests may rely on it ending with the private session |
| L1-15 | Live-session isolation of the probe | tool | box | none | `shared_x_property_before: "_AT_SPI_BUS:  no such atom on any window."`, `shared_x_property_unchanged: true`, `a11y_socket_under_private_runtime_dir: true` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: the shared X root has no `_AT_SPI_BUS` property on GNOME 46, so the adapter cannot locate the bus from the X11 root property and must use `org.a11y.Bus.GetAddress` |
| L1-16 | Behaviour with `toolkit-accessibility` false | dbus | box | §3.1: GNOME hint to check `toolkit-accessibility` | private session (own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `GSETTINGS_BACKEND=keyfile` under a private `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; the `memory` backend is per process, so `gsettings set` never reached the launcher and the first run read the real dconf), probe-owned `gnome-calculator`, release binary `status`, one run per direction. false: `private gsettings: false`, `GetAddress` ok, `IsEnabled: (False,)`, `registry child_count=2 names=['xdg-desktop-portal-gtk', 'gnome-calculator'] found_calc=True waited_s=0.5`, `status`: `"accessibility":{"state":"granted"}`. true: `private gsettings: true`, `IsEnabled: (True,)`, same registry and `granted`. Live session afterwards: `gsettings get ... toolkit-accessibility` `true`, `/run/user/1000/at-spi/bus_0` present | NEW-EDGE | 3.1: `toolkit-accessibility=false` does not stop `GetAddress`, app registration or the registry read; a GTK4 app registers either way and detection stays granted, so the setting is not a bus fault and `IsEnabled` is not read as a diagnostic. The GNOME hint names the setting only as the toggle behind the Settings accessibility switch, never as the cause of a failed detection. Scope: GTK4 app on a non-GNOME-Shell private session |
| L1-17 | Wayland session, nested or headless `gnome-shell`, `ydotool` | tool | box | Display Server Detection / Minimum OS: "Wayland: `ydotool` installed" | `wayland_feasibility.gnome_shell_help_flags: [--headless, --nested, --no-x11, --virtual-monitor, --wayland, --wayland-display, --x11]`, `gdm_wayland_enabled_conf: ["WaylandEnable=false"]`, `wayland_sessions_installed: [ubuntu-wayland.desktop, ubuntu.desktop]`, `uinput.mode: 0o600`, `uinput.writable_by_probe_user: false`, `uinput.groups: [adm, sudo]`, `tools.ydotoold.status: absent`, `tools.weston.version: weston 13.0.0`; only flag presence was recorded, no Wayland session was started; a headless `gnome-shell --headless --wayland` started in a private session with a restart loop and a health gate, and RemoteDesktop keys reached a native GTK4 entry (L28-21) | NEW-EDGE | a headless Wayland shell now starts from the tests, so only the `ydotool` half stays open and it is owned by L5-2 (closure: none needed here); capture and clipboard use of the same shell belongs to 3.10 |
| L1-18 | Flatpak and Qt toolchain | tool | box | §3.1: "Flatpak/Snap: Ensure the app has `--talk-name=org.a11y.Bus` permission" | `tools.flatpak.status: absent`, `python_modules.PyQt6.status: absent`, `python_modules.PyQt5.status: absent`, `python_modules.PySide6.status: absent`; snap is present (`tools.snap.version: snap          2.77.1`) | DEFERRED | needs: flatpak install (root); closure: 3.14 (sandbox guidance, with L12-9). The Qt identity half is answered: no Qt binding is needed to measure it, a launched Qt 6 app answers in L3-9 and L3-10 |
| L1-19 | Python access to AT-SPI | gi-atspi | box | §3.0: "pyatspi2 where it clarifies" | `python_modules.Atspi.version: typelib ok, desktops=1`, `python_modules.pyatspi.status: importable` (path under the user-space tree only), `packages_absent` includes `python3-pyatspi`, `python_modules.dbus.version: 1.3.2` | NEW-EDGE | 3.0 closure: pyatspi exists only through the user-space tree and `python-dbus` exists but the probes use Gio; the §3.0 wording is addressed by the KTD1 rewrite |

## Area L2 — trees and read cost

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L2-1 | Raw tree dump of GNOME Files, Terminal, Settings (frame-rooted walk, all nodes, 8-9 calls per node) | dbus | app | P3-O1: "`snapshot` on Ubuntu GNOME returns valid tree for Files, Terminal, Settings" | `02-tree-files.json analysis.node_count: 201`, `max_depth: 27`; terminal `89`, `max_depth: 8`; settings `215`, `max_depth: 22`; `call_errors: 0`, `nodes_with_errors: 0` in all three; Calculator `98` (`max_depth: 16`), Text Editor `69` (`max_depth: 17`) | CONFIRMS | 3.2/3.4: the raw tree is fully readable with the planned call set; no read failure on any node |
| L2-2 | Cycle and duplicate-reach detection with an ancestor-path set | dbus | api-contract | §3.2: "same cycle-guard discipline as macOS/Windows (ancestor-path set, not a global visited set)" | `analysis.cycle_count: 0`, `duplicate_reach_nodes: 0`, `null_children: 0` for all 6 apps (VS Code 3 variants too); `parent_mismatch: 1` Files, `2` Terminal, `1` Text Editor, `0` Calculator/Settings/VS Code (child's `Parent` property differs from the node it was reached from) | NEW-EDGE | 3.2: keep the ancestor guard, but this box never triggers it, so its failing direction is unmeasured (a unit test with a synthetic cycle is the only evidence); do not rely on `Parent` for re-identification, it disagreed with the walked parent on 4 nodes; 3.5: re-identification descends from the verified window frame and never climbs `Parent` |
| L2-3 | `ChildCount` property against `GetChildren` length | dbus | api-contract | none | `analysis.child_count_mismatch: 0` in every capture (6 apps) | NEW-EDGE | 3.2: `ChildCount` can be used to size/skip a `GetChildren` call; it agreed on every node read |
| L2-4 | Sequential read cost of a full walk (`call_sync`) | dbus | app | §3.2: "AT-SPI2 has no single 'copy multiple attributes' call ... concurrent-D-Bus-call batching strategy" | `timing.walk_sequential_call_sync` min/median/max ms: Files 421.9/434.8/458.4 (201 nodes); Terminal 137.6/141.7/163.6 (89); Calculator 191.0/196.0/206.6 (98); Settings 427.6/429.7/454.1 (215); Text Editor 149.5/160.1/173.6 (69); VS Code 424.3/431.8/459.1 (252). About 2.1 ms per node at 9 calls (0.2-0.3 ms per call) | NEW-EDGE | 3.2: baseline for the concurrency gain; a 2,000-node tree costs about 4 s sequentially |
| L2-5 | Concurrent walk, async `Gio.DBusConnection.call` under one main loop, 16 and 128 in flight | dbus | app | §3.2: "`futures::join_all` over property-get calls" stands in for a batch call | `walk_concurrent_async_inflight_128` min/median/max: Files 118.7/123.4/148.0, `speedup_vs_sync_median: 3.52`; Terminal 50.2/51.5/53.6, `2.75`; Calculator 67.9/79.5/95.6, `2.47`; Settings 119.4/123.1/125.8, `3.49`; Text Editor 43.8/52.2/60.5, `3.07`; VS Code 159.8/165.5/173.2, `2.61`. In-flight 16 is within 5-15% of 128 | CONFIRMS | 3.2: concurrent calls give a 2.5-3.5x walk speedup; a cap of 16 to 128 in flight is enough (no LimitsExceeded or timeouts seen, `call_errors: 0`) |
| L2-6 | `Properties.GetAll` against individual `Get` for the Accessible properties (Name, Description, ChildCount, Parent, AccessibleId) | dbus | api-contract | §3.2: "AT-SPI2 has no single 'copy multiple attributes' call" | `property_read_styles` (Files, 201 refs) median ms: `sync_individual_gets: 189.76`, `sync_getall: 57.62`, `async_individual_gets_inflight_128: 48.92`, `async_getall_inflight_128: 18.80`; same ordering on Terminal (66.4/21.8/19.8/10.9), Calculator (86.0/25.8/22.1/9.0), Settings (187.1/55.5/49.5/20.0), VS Code (170.2/69.4/56.0/36.4) | CONTRADICTS | 3.2: use one `GetAll(org.a11y.atspi.Accessible)` per node for the property group (3.3x fewer round trips than five `Get`s), then concurrency on top |
| L2-7 | `org.a11y.atspi.Cache.GetItems` as whole-app batch read | dbus | app | §3.2: "AT-SPI2 has no single 'copy multiple attributes' call ... so this sub-phase designs the concurrent-D-Bus-call batching strategy" | GTK4: `cache_getitems.supported: true`, Files `items_app_wide: 208`, `get_items_timing` 16.54/16.68/17.24 ms (vs 118.7 ms concurrent walk), `role_mismatch: 0`, `name_mismatch: 0`, `state_mismatch: 0`, `child_count_mismatch: 0` over `compared: 201`; Calculator 7.97/7.99/8.30 ms (98 nodes), Settings 18.47/18.67/20.24 ms, Text Editor 5.89/5.96/6.00 ms. GTK3 Terminal: `supported: false, error: org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.UnknownMethod`. VS Code (Chromium): same `UnknownMethod`. Cache item tuple length `10` (ref, app, parent, index, child count, interfaces, name, role, description, states): no extents, attributes, relations or NActions | CONTRADICTS | 3.2: add `Cache.GetItems` as the GTK4 fast path (about 7x faster than the concurrent walk), keep concurrent calls as the general path (GTK3, Chromium, Electron answer UnknownMethod) and still fetch extents/attributes/relations per node |
| L2-8 | Cache coverage against the walk | dbus | app | none | Files: `in_walk_not_in_cache: 0`, `in_cache_under_frame_not_in_walk: 5`; Calculator `0`/`2`; Settings `0`/`22`; Text Editor `0`/`3` (cache holds items parented under the frame that `GetChildren` did not return) | NEW-EDGE | 3.2: if the cache path is used, reconcile against `GetChildren` order; the extra items are unreachable by a children walk and must not become refs |
| L2-9 | Wrapper role names that carry no meaning, GTK4 apps | dbus | app | §3.4: "non-semantic wrapper elements with AT-SPI roles `ROLE_PANEL`, `ROLE_SECTION`, or `ROLE_FILLER` that have empty `Name` AND empty `Value`" | GTK4 captures have `wrapper_nodes: 0` yet `analysis.empty_name_roles`: Files 78 `generic` nodes with empty name of 201, Settings `generic: 78`, Calculator `generic: 29`, Text Editor `generic: 38`; no `panel`, `section` or `filler` role in any GTK4 capture | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: `is_web_wrapper` as specified skips nothing in GTK4 apps; decide whether `generic` (and empty-name `group`) join the skip set, only for web content or for all trees. Revisited by numeric role in L3-3: GTK4 `generic` is role number 39 (panel) and is the majority of GTK4 nodes |
| L2-10 | Depth-skip effect on VS Code at the product default depth (`max_depth` 10 in `crates/core/src/tree/tree_options.rs`) | dbus | app | §3.4 exit: "a VS Code snapshot at default depth finds 50+ refs through web-aware depth-skip alone (no force flag)" | VS Code plain launch (no env, no flag): `depth_skip_table["10"]`: `nodes_raw: 23` / `nodes_skip: 386`, `refable_by_role_raw: 4` / `refable_by_role_skip: 83` (second plain launch: `21`/`252`, `4`/`54`); `wrapper_nodes: 160` of 386; `max_depth: 33` raw, `max_effective_depth: 8`. Ref-ability here is an approximation (interactive role names), not the product rule | CONFIRMS | 3.4: skipping alone clears the 50-ref bar (54-83 against 4 without it); re-check with the real ref rule in the 3.4 test |
| L2-11 | Wrapper roles in Chromium content | dbus | app | §3.4: roles `ROLE_PANEL`, `ROLE_SECTION`, `ROLE_FILLER` | VS Code `roles`: `section: 164`, `panel: 12`, `filler` absent; `wrapper_candidates_with_value_iface: 0`; `empty_name_roles` `section: 149`, `panel: 11`; `wrapper_candidates_named: 15` (named wrappers must keep their depth cost) | CONFIRMS | 3.4: `section` is the dominant wrapper role in Chromium; `filler` never appears there but is the GTK3 wrapper (L2-12) |
| L2-12 | Depth-skip applied to a GTK3 tree | dbus | app | §3.4: depth-skip described for "Electron/WebKitGTK content" | Terminal (GTK3): `roles` `filler: 18, panel: 4`, `wrapper_nodes: 22` of 89; `max_depth: 8` raw, `max_effective_depth: 3`; `depth_skip_table["3"]`: `nodes_raw: 28` / `nodes_skip: 89`, `refable_by_role_raw: 14` / `refable_by_role_skip: 51` | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: the same rule changes GTK3 snapshots a lot (all 89 nodes inside depth 3 instead of 28); state whether the skip is global or limited to Chromium/WebKit subtrees |
| L2-13 | Chromium detection by process name | dbus | app | §3.4: "process name matching (electron, chrome, chromium, code)" | VS Code `registry_app.proc`: `comm: code`, `exe_basename: code`, `argv0_basename: code`, registry `name: code` | CONFIRMS | 3.4: `code` matches by `/proc/<pid>/comm` and exe; `electron` was not observed (VS Code does not report it); Chrome rows in L2-17 |
| L2-14 | Chromium accessibility activation by env variable or flag | dbus | box | §3.4: "Linux Chromium additionally respects `ACCESSIBILITY_ENABLED=1` as an alternative to `--force-renderer-accessibility`" | `02-tree-vscode.json` variants `None` (env var removed, no flag), `env` (`ACCESSIBILITY_ENABLED=1`), `flag` (`--force-renderer-accessibility`) all expose `document web` at the first poll (`stabilisation_series[0].document_nodes: 1`) and settle at 252/252/252 nodes (the first plain launch settled at 386 with extra window content). `a11y_status.IsEnabled: true` on this box. Failing direction measured in L16-7 on a private bus with `IsEnabled` false | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: with `IsEnabled` true neither is needed; with it false only the environment variable made Chrome register reliably and the flag alone did not (L16-7), so the guidance text names `ACCESSIBILITY_ENABLED=1` first |
| L2-15 | Lazy population of the Chromium tree after the window appears | dbus | app | none | VS Code plain `stabilisation_series`: `(t=0.01 s, 18 nodes)`, `(1.02 s, 18)`, `(2.21 s, 370)`, `(3.31 s, 386)`, then stable; env variant `23 -> 200 -> 252` over 2.3 s; Files/Terminal/Calculator/Settings/Text Editor are complete at the first poll (Files 194 -> 201 at 0.07 -> 1.12 s in an earlier run) | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: a snapshot taken right after launch sees 18-23 nodes in Electron; the observation needs a stable-count wait with a deadline, not a single read |

| L3-1 | Role domain: roles seen against the AT-SPI 2.52 enum | dbus | api-contract | §3.3 exit: "vocabulary conformance tests span every AT-SPI `Role` (complete mapping coverage, not a sample)" | `enum.role_last_defined: 130` (values 0-129, `last-defined` is a sentinel); `roles_seen_count: 44`, `roles_unseen_count: 86`, `role_number_not_in_enum: []` (every number a live app returned is a 2.52 enum value). Seen role numbers by count: panel (39) 418, section (85) 250, push-button (43) 254, label (29) 109, static (116) 100, notification (101) 49, menu (33) 43, menu-item (35) 37, image (27) 37, list-item (32) 33, plus 34 more down to 1 each. Unseen (86): see section 5 | CONFIRMS | 3.3: the domain to map is 130 values; the table needs an arm for every number 0-129 and a fallthrough for numbers above 129 (a newer libatspi); the 86 unseen roles can only be tested by a synthetic unit test, no running app here returned them |
| L3-2 | `GetRoleName` strings against the numeric `GetRole`, per toolkit | dbus | api-contract | §3.3: "AT-SPI `Role` enum → unified role enum"; capability map `subrole`: "AT-SPI2 `role-name` + `state-set`" | Same number, different `GetRoleName` per toolkit (`roles_seen[...].role_name_strings_by_toolkit`): role 39 (panel) is `generic` on GTK4 (306 nodes) and `panel` on GTK3/Chromium/Gecko; role 49 (scroll-pane) is `generic` on GTK4; role 23 (frame) is `application` on all four GTK4 apps and `frame` elsewhere; role 43 (push-button) is `button` on GTK4 and `push button` elsewhere; role 7 (check-box) is `switch` on a GTK4 Settings node; role 55 (table) `grid`; role 87 (form) `search`; role 61 (text) `text box` on GTK4 and `text` on Qt; role 54 (status-bar) `statusbar` on Chromium. `GetRoleName` for a GTK4 node is an ARIA-style label, not the enum nick. Role numbers were never ambiguous | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: key `roles.rs` on the numeric `GetRole`, never on `GetRoleName`; `subrole` from `role-name` is a toolkit-local vocabulary (GTK4 `generic`, `switch`, `search`, `grid` carry information the number does not, but are not portable), so state which toolkit strings are matched |

No attribute carries it (`attributes_tristate: answered_empty` on all 22) and the Name never does (`known_id_surfaces` finds only the explicit identifier by exact match) | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: for Qt `native_id` is `AccessibleId` directly, with no fallback read; keep the full dotted value (the last segment is the objectName), treat a class-name tail (`QPushButton`, `QAction`) and row-level duplicates as non-identifying; 3.5: rows that share the id are told apart by stable text, then by stored size, and stay ambiguous when nothing separates them |
| L3-10 | Identifier stability across an app restart | dbus | app | P2-O8: "controls with explicit IDs survive re-drills; controls without one continue through the fingerprint fallback" | Two launches each (`restart_comparison`): Qt `accessible_id_values: [17, 17, 17]` (all 17 distinct values identical), `same_ipath_role_name: 22` of 22; Calculator `98` of 98 and Files `201` of 201 identical by (index path, role, name) with no ids at all; VS Code `attr_id_values_run1_run2_both: [10, 9, 8]` (the differing ones are `list_id_1_0`, `list_id_4_0`, `workbench.panel.chat`, dynamic content), node counts 404 then 270 at settle, `fraction_ipath_role_name_of_min: 0.9407` | CONFIRMS | 3.3 implements the Qt `AccessibleId` and web DOM id as `native_id`, which survive restarts; GTK4 trees of a fresh launch are reproducible by path+role+name, so the fingerprint fallback has a stable basis, which 3.5 consumes; VS Code ids with list indices or lazily mounted parts do not survive; 3.5 resolves GTK elements by role, name and path with a stored-size tie-break |

GTK3 check menu item (Terminal `Show Menubar`) reads `checked`. Chromium (VS Code): toggle button (role 62) reads `pressed`, check box reads `checked`, entry carries attribute `placeholder`. Files search box (role 79 `search box`, opened by the search toggle): `Name` and `Description` empty, attributes only `toolkit`, no placeholder key; no GTK4 entry in Files, Text Editor or Calculator exposes placeholder text | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: for role 62 emit `checked` from either `pressed` or `checked` and `pressed` only for push buttons; GTK4 has no placeholder source, so nothing is emitted; `expanded` has no GTK4 source and the open state of a menu button is the toggle's `pressed`, which 3.7 owns together with expand/collapse by action name |
| L3-26 | Localized role and action names off-English (Calculator, Files, Terminal and Qt launched with `LANG` and `LC_ALL` set to `es_ES.UTF-8` and `LANGUAGE=es`) | dbus | app | capability map `role_description` | The apps' own text is localized (Calculator window name `Calculadora`, Files sidebar `Carpeta personal`), yet `GetLocalizedRoleName` equals `GetRoleName` for every role present: Calculator 9 distinct role/name pairs, Files 16, Terminal 12, Qt 12, zero differences; GTK4 role names stay English (`toggle button`, `grid cell`) and GTK3 and Qt stay English too | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: `GetLocalizedRoleName` carries no information beyond `GetRoleName` on any toolkit measured, so no per-node read is added and `role_description` comes only from the Chromium `roledescription` attribute |
| L3-27 | `VISIBLE` without `SHOWING` against node extents, per toolkit | dbus | app | §3.3: `offscreen` from the state set; L3-7 and L6-9 | Calculator 0 of 98, Files 0 of 367, Text Editor 0 of 74, Qt 0 of 18 (GTK4 and Qt never set `VISIBLE` without `SHOWING`). GTK3 Terminal: 33 of 49, every one a node of a closed menu with extents `[-2147483648,-2147483648,1,1]`. VS Code: 12 of 402, extents such as `[-12947,912,327,19]` and `[945,-49820,1,1]` plus one with extents `[542,619,304,35]` wholly inside the window frame `[40,112,1200,800]`. Firefox: 886 of 946, of which 173 have extents inside the window frame `[4,4,1152,921]` (menu bar and menus, the scroll pane, internal frames and both `document web` nodes). The word order is confirmed: `SHOWING` (bit 25) is in word 0 on every node and `HAS_POPUP` (bit 42) in word 1 | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: `offscreen` is not produced from the state set, because on-screen nodes read `VISIBLE` without `SHOWING` on Chromium and Gecko; the `showing` bit stays available; 3.6 owns the derivation (extents against the window frame and scroll container, the INT32_MIN sentinel as unknown bounds) |
| L3-28 | Chromium notification role (49 nodes in L3-1) | dbus | app | none | VS Code role 101 nodes read attributes `xml-roles: alert`, `live: assertive`, `container-live: assertive`, `atomic: true`, `class: monaco-alert`, states `enabled sensitive visible` (no `showing`) | CONFIRMS | 3.3: role 101 maps to `alert`, matching its source ARIA role |
| L3-29 | `EDITABLE` on text roles (60 terminal, 61 text, 79 entry) | dbus | api-contract | none | GTK4 text box (role 61): Calculator input `editable focusable focused multi-line`, Text Editor view `editable`; Calculator display `read-only` without `editable`; Qt line edit (role 61 `text`) `editable`; GTK3 Terminal (role 60) `enabled focusable sensitive showing visible`, no `editable` and no `read-only`; Chromium entry (role 79) `editable`; Files search box (role 79) `editable` | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: text roles 60 and 61 map to `textfield` only with `EDITABLE`, so the GTK3 Terminal resolves to `document` and the Calculator display to `document`; role 79 is a text field whatever its state |
| L3-30 | `Name` of GTK4 popover menu items | dbus | app | §3.3: `NameEvidence` slots | Menu items (role 35) in the Files, Text Editor and Calculator main-menu popovers have an empty `Name` and `Description` on all 8, 11 and 6 items, an `Action` interface with `NActions` 1, no `Text` interface, and one child (role 39 `generic`) with an empty name and no children of its own; the Zoom buttons (role 43) in the same popovers do name themselves (`Zoom out`). No node under the item carries its label text | NEW-EDGE | 3.3: nothing in the item's subtree to read, so the name resolves absent and no `child_label` source is built; a GTK4 popover item is addressable by position and action count only, which 3.4's dogfood on Files and Calculator must state for the agent |

| L4-2 | Click on GTK4 Calculator buttons (1 + 2 = C) by DoAction(0) | dbus | app | §3.7 Click: "`DoAction(0)`" | calculator rows: `returned: true`, display text `''` -> `'1'` -> `'1+'` -> `'1+2'` -> `'3'` -> `''`, window pixel md5 changes each step (independent X11 read), `x11_target_active_after: false`, `pointer_moved: false`; the single action is named `Click` | CONFIRMS | none |
| L4-3 | Action-less and unreported-effect nodes in GTK4 | dbus | app | §3.7 Click: "DoAction(0)" with no precondition | Text Editor buttons `Open`, `View`, `Main menu` carry the Action interface with an empty action list (their toggle-button twins carry `Click`); Settings sidebar rows are `button` nodes with `actions: []` (`sound_row_actions: []`); Files `Back`: `DoAction(0)` -> `returned: false` (no effect); Files grid cell `listitem.scroll-to`: `returned: true`, states unchanged | NEW-EDGE | 3.7: read the action list (`GetActions`/`NActions`) before `DoAction`; an empty list, or `false`, is `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`/not delivered, never a click |
| L4-4 | Action naming across toolkits | dbus | api-contract | §3.7 Toggle: "`Action.DoAction("toggle")` or `Action.DoAction("click")`" | D-Bus `DoAction` takes an index only (introspect: `DoAction` beside `GetActions`, `GetName`); names must be resolved via `GetActions`. GTK3 names are capitalised: check box/toggle button/radio `Click`, switch `Toggle`, expander/entry/spin `Activate`, combo `Press`; GTK4 mixes `Click` with GAction names such as `menu.popup`, `win.close`, `list.select-all` (census: 53/28/70/19 distinct names in Text Editor/Calculator/Files/Settings) | NEW-EDGE | 3.7: resolve action index by case-insensitive name from `GetActions`; do not assume lowercase verbs |

P3-O10 and the Text range read row are corrected in place |

P3-O10 is corrected in place |

ASCII `InsertText(5,', dear',6)` works | NEW-EDGE | 3.7: pass the UTF-8 byte length; never a code-point count or a value cutting a sequence; `true` is returned even when nothing was inserted, so read back |
