| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L5-1 | Version and path of the X11 input tool | tool | box | §3.8 Scope: "`xdotool key` (X11)"; Display Server Detection: "verify `xdotool` (X11) ... is installed" | `sections.env.tools.xdotool: {path: <user-space ad-tools root>/usr/bin/xdotool, version: "xdotool version 3.20160805.1"}` (2016 line, user-space extraction, not a system package) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: the install check must resolve the binary on PATH and report the version it found; do not assume a modern xdotool (`--window`, `type --delay`, `getwindowfocus` semantics differ across the 2016 and 3.2021 lines) |
| L5-2 | ydotool availability without root | tool | box | §3.8 Scope: "`ydotool key` (Wayland)"; P3-O3: "xdotool/ydotool" | `sections.env.tools.ydotool.path` resolves (user-space extraction), `version: null`; `sections.env.uinput: "crw------- 1 root root 10, 223"`, `uinput_rw_for_user: false` | DEFERRED | needs: a measurement of `ydotool` with a privileged `ydotoold` or a `/dev/uinput` writable by the test user, covering keys and pointer into a Wayland-native window and the cost of requiring root; closure: 3.15 (the input sub-phase built XTEST and RemoteDesktop keys instead; this route stays unbuilt, see `docs/phases.md` §3.15) |
| L5-3 | libei and RemoteDesktop reachability | tool | box | §3.8 libei note: "documented ... as a researched future alternative ... not adopted as a dependency" | `sections.env.libei: [libei.so.1, libei.so.1.2.1, libeis.so.1, libeis.so.1.2.1]` present as system libraries; `sections.env.session_bus_names` contains `org.gnome.Mutter.RemoteDesktop` and `org.freedesktop.portal.Desktop`; `xdg_session_type: "tty"`, `wayland_display: null` | DEFERRED | needs: RemoteDesktop access control measured on a live user session, where an ordinary client may be refused or asked for consent, because the private-bus runs (L13-16, L13-18, L28-9, L28-21) cannot show it; closure: 3.15 (its live-session re-run). The libei "not adopted" statement is not contradicted, the library is present |
| L5-4 | AT-SPI DeviceEventController surface | dbus | api-contract | P3-O3: "all mouse commands via AT-SPI actions + xdotool/ydotool" (no mention of the registry synthesis methods) | `sections.env.dec_methods: [RegisterKeystrokeListener, DeregisterKeystrokeListener, GetKeystrokeListeners, GenerateKeyboardEvent, GenerateMouseEvent, NotifyListenersSync, NotifyListenersAsync]` on `org.a11y.atspi.Registry` `/org/a11y/atspi/registry/deviceeventcontroller` | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: `GenerateKeyboardEvent(i,s,u)` and `GenerateMouseEvent(i,i,s)` exist as a no-subprocess alternative on X11; their effect on apps is not measured here (L5-6) |
| L5-5 | Does each pointer synthesis path move the real cursor | tool + dbus + gi-atspi | api-contract | Core invariant 1: "run without ... physical cursor movement unless `--headed` explicitly opts into cursor input"; §3.8: "raw cursor commands require `--headed`" | `pointer_only.results.xdotool_mousemove.all_arrived: true`, `gi_generate_mouse_event_abs.all_arrived: true`, `raw_dbus_GenerateMouseEvent_abs.all_arrived: true` (8 of 8 each; independent XQueryPointer read-back equals the requested coordinates); `gi_rel_7_-3.delta: [7,-3]`, `raw_rel_7_-3.delta: [7,-3]`. Latency to arrival, min/median/max ms (n=7 after discarded warm-up): xdotool `1.6/1.7/2.4` (includes process spawn), gi `0.1/0.1/0.3`, raw D-Bus `0.2/0.3/0.3`. No button state changed (`buttons: []`) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: every X11 synthetic pointer path, AT-SPI included, moves the user's real cursor, so no path is headless; the headed-only policy placement for `hover`, `drag`, `mouse-*` is correct and the AT-SPI route is no exception |
| L5-6 | Precondition for keystroke synthesis: is the target window really focused | tool + gi-atspi | box | Core invariant 1 / §3.8 (no claim about focus readiness) | `gates[0..6].ewmh_active_is_target: true` and `x_focus` equals the target xid while `frame_active_state: false` for 15 s (`frame_active_wait_ms: 15078`); `activate.tried`: `wmctrl-ia` rc 0 but not active after 3 s, `xdotool-windowactivate` same, `xdotool-windowfocus` set EWMH active; `grabs: {keyboard_already_grabbed: true, pointer_already_grabbed: true}`; `pointer_only.active_window: 0` (no active window at all) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW == target` is not sufficient evidence that keys will land there; check the AT-SPI frame `active` state before synthesizing and fail with a clear error instead of typing blind. Cause on this box was a shell system access dialog pending since the portal probes (see L9-13), which holds the keyboard and pointer grabs; keystrokes synthesized through XTEST then go to the grab holder, not the focused window |
| L5-7 | Keyboard and chord synthesis (key, ctrl+a, shift+arrows), type with é ü 你好 emoji, 2,000-char string, xdotool and AT-SPI paths | tool + gi-atspi + dbus | app | §3.8 Scope, API Mapping Keyboard row | key, `ctrl+a` and non-ASCII `type` measured through XTEST on GTK3, GTK4, Qt, Text Editor and Calculator with the grab cleared (L28-4, L28-12, L28-13, L28-14, L28-15); shift+arrows and a 2,000-character string were not driven | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: the keyboard bullet is built on XTEST and read back by the application; shift+arrows and the long string are accepted unmeasured live, because the plan tests cover the Shift wrapper and the 32-character chunking |
| L5-8 | Mouse click, double-click, right-click, wheel, drag inside a probe window; xdotool and AT-SPI paths; read back through AT-SPI Text | tool + gi-atspi + dbus | app | §3.8 Scope, API Mapping Mouse and Scroll rows | click, wheel and drag measured through XTEST on GTK3, GTK4, Qt and Files with the effect read back (L28-14); double-click and right-click are covered by plan tests only | CONFIRMS | 3.8: the mouse and scroll rows are built on XTEST; the AT-SPI `GenerateMouseEvent` path is not used |
| L5-9 | Long-press (button hold timing) | tool | app | capability map `LongPress`: "Coordinate via `ydotool/xdotool`" (P3-O13: "timed `xdotool/ydotool` button-hold") | not driven: core has no `LongPress` action, so no route is built for it | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: accepted, no owner needed until core adds the action; the held button a drag keeps is released by the guard |
| L5-10 | `ForceClick` | dbus + tool | api-contract | capability map `ForceClick`: "Not natively supported — return `ActionNotSupported`" | no measurement attempted; X11 has no pressure input primitive in the tools measured (`xdotool`, DEC methods list in L5-4 carry no pressure argument) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: keep `ActionNotSupported`; the evidence is the absence of a pressure parameter in the `GenerateMouseEvent(i,i,s)` signature and in xdotool's mouse verbs, not a behavioural test |
| L5-11 | `xdotool --window` (XSendEvent) delivery to a focused and to a background window | tool | app | none | not used: the route is XTEST, which follows the active window and not a window id (L28-1) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: accepted, `xdotool` is not a dependency |
| L5-12 | Wayland input (ydotool, RemoteDesktop portal, libei) | tool | box | §3.8 Scope: "`ydotool key` (Wayland)" | not measured in this area; Wayland input is measured in L13-13 to L13-18 | DEFERRED | needs: as L5-2, the `ydotool` route on Wayland; RemoteDesktop keys were measured without it (L28-21); closure: 3.15 |

## Area L6 — hit-testing and occlusion

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L6-1 | Introspect the Component interface on a live node and call the point-test methods | dbus | api-contract | §3.6 Key APIs: "`org.a11y.atspi.Component.ContainsPoint`" | `toolkits.gtk3.cases.scroll.component_methods_introspected: [Contains, GetAccessibleAtPoint, GetExtents, GetPosition, GetSize, GetLayer, GetMDIZOrder, GrabFocus, GetAlpha, SetExtents, SetPosition, SetSize, ScrollTo, ScrollToPoint]` (identical for gtk4). There is no `ContainsPoint`; the method is `Contains(x i, y i, coord_type u) -> b` | CONTRADICTS | 3.6: call `Component.Contains` and `Component.GetAccessibleAtPoint`; fix the §3.6 Key APIs line |
| L6-2 | `Contains` on the same button visible, covered by a second window, and with its window minimized (GTK3, screen and window coords) | dbus | api-contract | §3.6: "`hit_test` three-way result via `Component.ContainsPoint` + bounds corroboration" | visible: `cases.visible_button.self.screen_center.contains.value: true`; covered (`cases.covered_button.x11_occluders_at_button_center.covering_is_probe_cover: true`, cover index 10 above main index 9 in `_NET_CLIENT_LIST_STACKING`): `cases.covered_button.self.screen_center.contains.value: true`, window coords `true`; minimized (`x11_hidden_state_seen: true`): `cases.minimized_button.self.screen_center.contains.value: true`. Outside point (+40 px right of the button) `contains: false` in all three | CONFIRMS | 3.6: `Contains` is pure geometry, so a `true` never proves the point is unobstructed; map it to `Unknown`/geometric-yes and take the occlusion verdict from X11 stacking plus window geometry (L6-3). Never report "not hit" from `Contains` alone |
| L6-3 | X11 stacking as the occlusion source | tool | box | §3.6: "`hit_test` ... + bounds corroboration" (no source named for occlusion) | `cases.covered_button.stacking_main_index: 9`, `stacking_cover_index: 10`, `x11_occluders_at_button_center.covering_point_count: 1`, `covering_is_probe_cover: true`; after raising main: `cases.covered_button_after_raise_main.x11_occluders_at_button_center.covering_point_count: 0`. Both directions measured, both runs. AT-SPI `GetMDIZOrder` returned `0` for main and cover frames and `GetLayer` `7` (WINDOW) for both (`main_window_frame_snap`, `cover_window_snap`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: occlusion = windows above the target's toplevel in `_NET_CLIENT_LIST_STACKING` whose frame rectangle (client geometry plus `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS`) contains the point; do not use `GetMDIZOrder`/`GetLayer`, they carry no z-order here. Wayland has no equivalent measured: needs: Wayland session; closure: 3.6 |
| L6-4 | `GetAccessibleAtPoint` called on the main frame, on the covering window's frame and on the leaf button, at the covered point (GTK3) | dbus | api-contract | §3.6: "`hit_test` three-way result via `Component.ContainsPoint`" | on main frame: `cases.covered_button.via_main_window_frame.screen_center.at_point` is a `filler` (the direct child), not the button; on the leaf button: `self.screen_center.at_point.null: true`; on the cover frame: `via_cover_window_frame.at_point: label "COVER"` (cover app, other pid). Same shape uncovered (`visible_button.via_window_frame`) so the answer never reflects the cover for the covered window | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: `GetAccessibleAtPoint` returns the immediate child only (null ref on a leaf) and is per-window; to find the topmost element at a point, find the topmost window from stacking (L6-3) and descend its `GetAccessibleAtPoint` until a null ref; compare the result's bus name/path to the target |
| L6-5 | GTK3 extents in screen and window coords against X11 geometry | dbus | app | none | button `extents_screen [100,137,420,34]`, `extents_window [0,0,420,34]`; X11 client origin `[100,137]`, `frame_extents_lrtb [0,0,37,0]`. Frame node `window_frame_screen [100,100,420,457]` = X11 client origin minus the 37 px title bar, height `420+37`; `window_frame_window [0,-37,420,457]`. Widget screen coords equal X11 client origin plus window-relative offset | CONFIRMS | 3.6: widget screen extents are directly comparable with X11 client geometry; the frame node includes the title bar (offset `-_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS` top), so compare widgets against the client rectangle and only the frame node against the decorated rectangle |
| L6-6 | GTK4 extents in screen and window coords against X11 geometry | dbus | app | none | button `extents_screen [0,0,420,34]` (the widget size at origin 0,0 whatever the window position, while X11 client origin is `[100,137]`), `extents_window [17,5,420,34]` (an unexplained 17,5 offset; a 420-wide button at x=17 overflows the 420-wide window). The top-level node is role `application` with `extents_screen [0,0,420,420]`; no `frame` node. `Contains` at the screen centre of the extents returns `true` even 40 px beyond the right edge (`self.screen_outside.contains.value: true`) and `false` at the window-coord centre of its own window extents (`self.window_center.contains.value: false` at (227,22) inside `[17,5,420,34]`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: GTK4 4.14 Component geometry is not usable as truth under X11: screen extents ignore the window position, `Contains` disagrees with `GetExtents`. Treat every GTK4 `Contains`/extents hit-test as `Unknown` and corroborate from X11 geometry only if the window-coord offset can be explained; owner: 3.6 (measure GTK4.16+ and a Qt/Electron app) |
| L6-7 | Zero-size node: GTK3 DrawingArea requested 0x0 | dbus | app | §3.6: "zero-bounds/disabled/occluded fixture cases" | `toolkits.gtk3.cases.zero_size_named.snap.extents_screen: [100,177,1,1]` (not 0x0), `scanned_zero_size_count: 0` over 609 nodes; `Contains` at origin `true`, at origin+1 `false` | NEW-EDGE | 3.6/3.12: a GTK3 widget cannot present 0x0 extents (allocation reported as 1x1); the zero-bounds case must be sourced from GTK4 (L6-8) or another toolkit |
| L6-8 | Zero-size node in GTK4 (scan for `w==0 or h==0`) | dbus | app | §3.6: "zero-bounds/disabled/occluded fixture cases" | `toolkits.gtk4.tree.scanned_zero_size_count: 1`; node `role generic`, `extents_screen [0,0,0,0]`, `extents_window [0,40,0,0]`, states `sensitive, showing, visible`; `Contains(0,0,screen)` returns `true` for the 0x0 rectangle. The named 0x0 DrawingArea itself was not exposed (`zero_named_found: false`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: a 0x0 rectangle must map to `Unknown`/not-hit regardless of `Contains` (it answers `true` for an empty rectangle); 3.12: real GTK4 apps present such `generic` nodes, name one as the zero-bounds target |
| L6-9 | Offscreen list item (row 250 of 300, scrolled list), extents, `Contains`, states | dbus | app | §3.6: "Visibility/enabled/offscreen evidence feeding the core auto-wait gate" | GTK3: `cases.offscreen_row.snap.extents_screen: [-2147483648,-2147483648,420,22]` (INT32_MIN sentinel, also in window coords), states `enabled, focusable, sensitive, visible` with `showing` absent (visible row 1: `showing` present); `Contains` at (sentinel+210, sentinel+11) returns `true`; the same point via the window frame returns `contains: false`, `at_point.null: true`. GTK4: `extents_window [2,5526,420,22]`, `extents_screen [0,0,420,22]`, states include `showing` and `visible` | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: GTK3 offscreen = `SHOWING` absent with `VISIBLE` present and INT32_MIN extents; treat the sentinel as unknown bounds, never feed it to `Contains`/overlap math (it yields a false `true`). GTK4 reports `SHOWING` for an item 5,500 px outside the viewport, so `SHOWING` is not offscreen evidence on GTK4; offscreen must be derived by comparing window-coord extents to the scroll container |
| L6-10 | SHOWING / VISIBLE / ICONIFIED / ENABLED alongside each case (GTK3 and GTK4) | dbus | app | §3.6: "Visibility/enabled/offscreen evidence" | button visible, covered, and window minimized (GTK3): `snap.states.names` all contain `showing, visible, enabled, sensitive`; only the window frame changes on minimize (`cases.minimized_button.window_frame_snap.states.names` adds `iconified`; `atspi_window_iconified_seen: true`, X11 `_NET_WM_STATE_HIDDEN` seen). GTK4: `iconified` never appears (`atspi_window_iconified_seen: false` while `x11_hidden_state_seen: true`); GTK4 button states have `sensitive` but no `enabled` | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: covered and minimized do not clear `SHOWING` on descendants; minimized is only visible as `ICONIFIED` on a GTK3 frame or `_NET_WM_STATE_HIDDEN` on X11; use X11 for the minimized check on GTK4; enabled = `ENABLED` or (`SENSITIVE` where `ENABLED` is never emitted, as GTK4 4.14) |
| L6-11 | `Component.ScrollTo` / `ScrollToPoint` on GTK3 (row 250, three scroll types plus two point forms, independent adjustment read-back) | dbus | app | §3.6: "`scroll_into_view` — AT-SPI2 has no native scroll-into-view primitive" | both methods are present in introspection (`ScrollTo_in_introspection: true`, `ScrollToPoint_in_introspection: true`); every call returns `false`: `toolkits.gtk3.cases.scroll.attempts[*].return.value: false` (ANYWHERE=6, TOP_LEFT=0, TOP_EDGE=2, ScrollToPoint screen and window); `adjustment_changed: false` (app-printed vadjustment stayed `0.0`); control on an already-visible row `false`, on a button `false` | CONTRADICTS | 3.6: replace the "no native primitive" wording with the measured fact (below); implement the coordinate fallback as the working path |
| L6-12 | `Component.ScrollTo` on GTK4 (row 250) | dbus | app | §3.6: "`scroll_into_view` — AT-SPI2 has no native scroll-into-view primitive" | `toolkits.gtk4.cases.scroll.attempts[*].return.error` is `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.NotSupported` for ANYWHERE, TOP_LEFT, TOP_EDGE; same error on the visible-row and button controls; app vadjustment stayed `0.0`. `ScrollToPoint` was not called on GTK4 (no scroll-container node of role `scroll pane` was exposed to take a target point from) | CONFIRMS | 3.6: GTK4 4.14.5 gives `NotSupported` (an error to catch, not a `false`); treat both outcomes as "no primitive" and fall back |
| L6-13 | `GetPosition` as the locator for coordinate-based scroll | dbus | app | §3.6: "implement via `Component.GetPosition` + coordinate-based scroll synthesis" | GTK3 offscreen row has INT32_MIN position (L6-9), so it cannot aim a scroll; GTK4 gives a window-relative y (`extents_window[1]: 5526`) that is usable against the scroll container's window-relative extents but not against screen coords (L6-6) | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: for GTK3 offscreen items, locate the target from list order/sibling geometry (a visible neighbour's extents plus index delta) or scroll in steps until `SHOWING` appears; do not rely on `GetPosition`. needs: GTK4 coordinate frame explained; closure: 3.6 |
| L6-14 | `GetMDIZOrder`, `GetLayer`, `GetAlpha` on frames and widgets | dbus | api-contract | none | frames: `layer.value: 7`, `mdi_z.value: 0` for both windows; widgets: `layer 3`, `mdi_z 0`; every call answered (no error) in both toolkits | NEW-EDGE | 3.6: ignore these for occlusion; values are constants here (WINDOW/WIDGET layer, z 0) |

## Area L7 — events

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L7-1 | Do GTK4 apps emit AT-SPI events without any `RegisterEvent`? Phase A (match rules only, `registered_before: []`) vs B (registered) vs A2 (deregistered) | dbus | app | P3-O9: "implemented via `zbus::Proxy::receive_signal` on AT-SPI2 signals" (no registration step stated) | `A.calc_display_InsertText.target_event_misses: 0`, `B...: 0`, `A2...: 0`; same for value and DoAction triggers in all three phases; latencies equal within noise (insert min 3.46-3.55 ms) | CONFIRMS | 3.x (watch): adding a bus match rule is sufficient for GTK4 apps; calling `RegisterEvent` is optional (it succeeded: `register_result: ok` x10, `GetRegisteredEvents` read back keyed by the caller's unique name, removed by `DeregisterEvent`). Do not make correctness depend on it, but call it for non-GTK4 toolkits (not measured here) |
| L7-2 | Does `RegisterEvent` alone (no `AddMatch`) deliver anything? Phase C | dbus | api-contract | P3-O9: signal subscription via zbus | `C.*.target_event_misses: 2` of 2 on insert, delete, DoAction, both value triggers; `startup_event_count_after_launch: 0` | NEW-EDGE | 3.x (watch): the bus match rule is the delivery mechanism; registration without a match rule yields zero events (failing case), match rule without registration yields all (passing case, L7-1) |
| L7-3 | Programmatic value change: `Value.CurrentValue` set on a GTK4 spin button (Atspi `Set`) and in-app `set_value`; time to `Object:PropertyChange:accessible-value` | dbus | app | Testing: "`watch --event value-changed ... delivers an event within 500 ms` of a programmatic value change" | in-app: `min_ms 0.74, median 0.85, max 1.33`; via AT-SPI set: `min_ms 0.98, median 1.09, max 1.71` (A; B and A2 within 0.2 ms); `target_event_misses: 0`; read-back `CurrentValue` 10.0 / 40.0 as set; role `spin button`, ifaces `Value,Text,EditableText,Action` | CONFIRMS | 3.x (watch): map `value-changed` to `PropertyChange:accessible-value`. Same change also emits `TextChanged:delete` then `TextChanged:insert` before it (arrival order delete, insert, accessible-value), so a listener must filter on the detail string |
| L7-4 | Text change events: `EditableText.InsertText` / `DeleteText` on the calculator display | dbus | app | P3-O9 lists PropertyChange, ChildrenChanged, StateChanged, Window:* (no text events) | insert: `min_ms 3.49, median 3.65, max 4.14`; delete: `min_ms 0.63, median 0.65, max 0.88`; event set per run `Object:TextChanged:insert` x2 + `Object:TextCaretMoved` (delete: `TextChanged:delete` x2 + `TextCaretMoved`); duplicate `TextChanged` per change in every run; readback `GetText` "7" then "" | NEW-EDGE | 3.x (watch): subscribe to `TextChanged` (+`TextCaretMoved`); de-duplicate the doubled signal. First delete on the empty display is the warm-up miss (`target_event_misses: 1`, `n: 7`) |
| L7-5 | Button `Action.DoAction(0)` on a calculator key; time to `TextChanged` on the display | dbus | app | Testing: event within 500 ms of a programmatic change | `latency.all_ms` A `[50.8, 254.21, 53.01, 254.12, 50.62, 0.66, 0.99]`, `min_ms 0.66, median 50.8, max 254.21`; B max 254.98; A2 max 255.82; no misses; text read back "1" | NEW-EDGE | 3.x (watch) and 3.11 (wait): action-driven changes arrive in bimodal bursts (~0.2-1 ms, ~50 ms, ~254 ms) after the call; budget at least 300 ms of settle after an action, below the 500 ms bar but not by a wide margin Closed in 3.11: the waits poll to the deadline with a pause after each poll and the 300 ms is a floor and not a budget (L33-9, L33-11). |
| L7-6 | Second window open/close: fixture opens and destroys a second `Gtk.Window`; expect `Window:Create` / `Window:Destroy` | dbus | app | P3-O9: signals include "`Window:Create`, `Window:Destroy`" | open, 2 of 2 attempts in each of A, B, A2: `open_miss: 2`, `open_kinds: {ChildrenChanged:add, StateChanged:showing, StateChanged:visible, BoundsChanged}`; close: `close_miss: 2`, `close_kinds: {StateChanged:defunct}`; `opened_children_delta: 1`, `children_restored: true`. Registered phase B identical, so registration does not enable them | CONTRADICTS | 3.x (watch): window appearance = `ChildrenChanged:add` on the app root (+`StateChanged:showing`); disappearance = `StateChanged:defunct` on the old window (no `ChildrenChanged:remove` seen); see section 3 |
| L7-7 | Focus and activation events, measured with the probe window verified active (`xprop -root _NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` equals the fixture frame, frame `active` state bit read back true); in-app `grab_focus` between two entries, then `xdotool windowactivate` main <-> second probe window (both own); phases A (no register), B (`RegisterEvent`), A2 (deregistered) | dbus | app | P3-O9: signals include "`StateChanged:focused`" | active window: `inapp_focus_active_window.latency.min_ms` A 0.35 / B 0.37 / A2 0.37 (median 0.36-0.40, max 0.75-1.48), event `Object:StateChanged:focused` in all three, same without registration. Window swap (8 runs, 0 misses): events per swap `Object:StateChanged:active` x2, `Window:deactivate`, `Window:activate`, `Object:StateChanged:focused`; `window_activation_swap.latency.min_ms` A 4.89 / B 4.81 / A2 4.48 (median 6.15-8.88, max 9.0-10.63; includes the xdotool process time, so an upper bound). `Component.GrabFocus` on the two entries: `NotSupported` in all three phases. Earlier L7-7 misses (`target_event_misses: 2`, no events) came from a window that was not the active window | CONFIRMS | 3.x (watch): `StateChanged:focused` fires only while the toplevel is active; also subscribe `StateChanged:active` on the frame and `Window:activate`/`Window:deactivate` (these do fire on GTK4, with lowercase member names, no registration needed). First in-app focus attempt per phase is the warm-up miss (`target_event_misses: 1`, `n: 7`) |
| L7-8 | `Component.GrabFocus` on a GTK4 widget | dbus | app | none | `calc_Component.GrabFocus_alternating.errors: ["...DBus.Error.NotSupported:  (7)"]` in A, B, A2, C (4 of 4) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: focus cannot be moved through `Component.GrabFocus` on GTK4; use an action or the focus path of the adapter |
| L7-9 | Startup signal volume after launch (noise a listener must tolerate) | dbus | box | none | `startup_event_count_after_launch`: A 8, B 18, A2 8 (counted at a single point after tree discovery; C 0 only because there was no match rule); `BoundsChanged` bursts ~209 events over the open/close of one window | NEW-EDGE | 3.x (watch): filter by sender and member server-side or cheaply client-side; `BoundsChanged`, `StateChanged:showing/visible`, `PropertyChange:widget` dominate window churn |

## Area L8 — windows, apps, displays

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L8-1 | Enumerate registry apps, frames, pids | dbus | api-contract | §3.4: "list_windows/list_apps/focused_window via the window-manager-neutral AT-SPI2 Window interface" | `enumeration.registry_app_count: 19`. `Registry.GetChildren` gives (bus name, path) per app. Pid comes from `org.freedesktop.DBus.GetConnectionUnixProcessID` on the a11y bus (`apps[].pid`). Application interface props `Id`, `ToolkitName`, `Version` all read. Many apps (gsd-*, ibus-*) have `children: []`, which is empty-but-answered. Chrome lists 6 frame-role children (2 distinct `BrowserRootView` windows plus `RootView` bubbles), so list_windows needs filtering. Registry GetChildren min 0.288 ms. Full apps+frames enumeration min 36.7 ms, median 37.5, max 44.1 (19 apps). Pid lookups for all 19 apps min 1.94 ms. | CONFIRMS | 3.4: build list_apps from Registry children and pid from the bus daemon. Treat empty app children as zero windows. Dedupe or filter Chromium `RootView` frames. |
| L8-2 | Window roles and identity fields | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 (AT-SPI Window interface) | Probe GTK3 windows show `role_name: frame` for top-levels and `dialog` for the dialog. `interfaces: [Accessible, Component, Collection]`, so there is NO `Window` interface in the capture. A gnome-shell child is `window`. `attributes` keys are only `toolkit` and `window-type` (`atspi_window_attribute_keys`). `accessible_id` is empty. | CONTRADICTS | 3.4: window enumeration uses the Accessible/Component interfaces, not a Window interface; membership is a root child with `Component`, whatever its role (L41-1). |
| L8-3 | Focused window, AT-SPI vs `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` | dbus + tool | box | §3.4 focused_window | `focus.agrees: true`. After `xdotool` activation, X11 active equals the target and the target's AT-SPI frame carries `ACTIVE` (`atspi_active_frame_of_target`). The inactive probe app has no ACTIVE (`inactive_probe_has_ACTIVE: false`). Two accessibles report ACTIVE at once (`atspi_active_count: 2`): the real app frame and a role `application` child under the `mutter-x11-frames` app, which has the same title. | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: pick ACTIVE among `frame`/`dialog` roles only and ignore children of the frames helper app. Cross-check with `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` when X11. |
| L8-4 | AT-SPI field carrying an X11 window id | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 and §3.12 intro: "a recycled-identifier hazard" | `atspi_fields_carrying_xid: []`. Searched name, path, attributes, `accessible_id` and app `Id` for the decimal and hex XID of every probe window. None found. | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: AT-SPI to X11 mapping must be by pid plus title plus geometry, never by id. |
| L8-5 | Same-title windows | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 | Three windows share the title `adprobe-same` across two pids (`same_title_windows.title_alone_unique: false`). pid+title leaves 2 X11 candidates for the 2-window app (`n_candidates: 2`). Extents differ per window. Raw comparison to X geometry shows a mismatch (`extents_equal_xgeom: false`) because AT-SPI frame extents include the title bar. Example: AT-SPI `[100,120,320,237]` vs X `{x:100,y:157,w:320,h:200}`, with `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS "0, 0, 37, 0"`. Offline check (not computed by the script): window 2 is AT-SPI `[160,160,...]` vs X y=197, which is 160+37. | NEW-EDGE | 3.4: map by pid + title + extents, converting with `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS` (AT-SPI y = X y - top extent, h + top). Use child order or extents to disambiguate same-title windows. |
| L8-6 | X11 window properties | tool | box | §3.4 "falling back to wmctrl" | `xprop` gives `_NET_WM_PID`, `_NET_WM_NAME`, `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE` (NORMAL vs DIALOG), `_NET_WM_STATE`, `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS`, `WM_TRANSIENT_FOR`, `WM_CLIENT_MACHINE`. `_NET_CLIENT_LIST` read costs min 1.94 ms. `wmctrl.present: false` on this box (not installed). `xwininfo` gives absolute geometry. | CONTRADICTS | 3.4: the fallback must not depend on wmctrl. Use xprop/_NET_CLIENT_LIST (or Gdk/x11 reads) directly. |
| L8-7 | XID reuse risk | tool | box | §3.12 intro: "Linux gains an equivalent only if the same recycled-identifier hazard is measured on X11 or Wayland" | Same process, 150 create/destroy cycles: `same_process.reused_ids: 0`, ids step by 4 (`0x4000003, 0x4000007, ...`). Fresh process per window, 40 spawns: `fresh_process_per_window.distinct_xids: 1, reused: 39`, every first window is `0x4000003`. Overlapping live processes got different bases. | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the hazard is measured on X11, across processes not within one process. An XID is not a stable window identity across a process restart. Any stored window identity must pair the XID with pid and the process start time. 3.12 owns the field.; 3.5: the window gate pairs the id with the stored process instance and the bus owner pid, so a reused id is refused |
| L8-8 | Window ops with xdotool | tool | box | API Mapping "Window ops": `xdotool` / `wmctrl` for resize, move, minimize, maximize, restore | `xdotool` move+resize read back `{x:150,y:197,w:410,h:260}` (`took_effect: true`, rc 0). `xdotool windowminimize` sets `_NET_WM_STATE_HIDDEN` and WM_STATE Iconic, and AT-SPI shows `ICONIFIED` on the frame. xdotool on a nonexistent id: rc 1 with `BadWindow`. AT-SPI `Component.SetExtents` with signature `(iiiiu)` was rejected with `InvalidArgs` (`took_effect: false`); other signatures not tried. | CONFIRMS (partial) | 3.4: keep xdotool for move, resize and minimize, and read back through X11 state. Do not use SetExtents on a GTK3 frame. |
| L8-9 | wmctrl and maximize/restore | tool | box | API Mapping "Window ops" | `wmctrl` is not installed (rc 127). Every wmctrl step records `rc: 127` and `took_effect: false` (maximize, restore, activate-from-minimize). Maximize, restore and unminimize were not measured. | DEFERRED | needs: wmctrl (or another EWMH client-message sender) extracted into the user-space tool root, then measure maximize, restore, unminimize in both directions; closure: 3.9 (maximize, restore and unminimize measured in L11-20 and L11-22) |
| L8-10 | Display geometry and scale | tool + dbus | box | §3.4: "list_displays via XRandR (X11) / the Wayland portal's equivalent + per-monitor scale" | `xrandr --listmonitors`: 1 monitor `Virtual1 1280/339x800/212+0+0`. `xrandr --query` shows 0mm x 0mm. Mutter `GetCurrentState`: one logical monitor `scale: 1.0`, `primary: true`, props `layout-mode: 2`, `global-scale-required: True`, `legacy-ui-scaling-factor: 1`. Mutter supports per-mode `supported_scales` ([1.0], [1.0,2.0,3.0], ...). Gdk `scale_factor: 1`, workarea `[66,32,1214,768]`. `Xft.dpi: 96`. Timing: Mutter min 0.529 ms, median 0.635, max 1.879; `xrandr --query` subprocess min 3.02 ms, median 3.09, max 4.92. Single display only, so multi-monitor and mixed scale are unmeasured. | CONFIRMS | 3.4: list_displays from XRandR/Mutter works. Prefer the Mutter DisplayConfig `scale` for the per-display `scale`. xrandr gives no scale. Report workarea separately from monitor geometry. |
| L8-11 | Wayland-side window listing fallbacks | dbus | box | §3.4: "falling back to wmctrl/xdg-desktop-portal introspection" | `portal_has_window_listing: false`: no `org.freedesktop.portal.*` interface name contains window or toplevel (`portal_interfaces` lists 31 names: Inhibit, Screenshot, ScreenCast, RemoteDesktop, Clipboard, ...). `org.gnome.Shell.Introspect.GetWindows` returns `AccessDenied`: "GetWindows is not allowed". | CONTRADICTS | 3.4 (Wayland path): there is no portal or shell route to enumerate windows. Window enumeration under Wayland must come from AT-SPI alone. |
| L8-12 | Display Server Detection | tool | box | "Display Server Detection": WAYLAND_DISPLAY then DISPLAY then PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED | On this X11 session `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is unset, `DISPLAY=:0`, rule yields x11 (`env.display_server_detection.phases_rule_result: x11`). The ssh environment reports `XDG_SESSION_TYPE: tty`, so that variable is not a usable signal. Wayland and Xwayland cases not measured. | CONFIRMS (X11 branch only) | 3.x: do not use XDG_SESSION_TYPE. The Wayland-with-Xwayland case (both variables set) belongs to the Wayland probe area. |

## Area L9 — capture and clipboard

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L9-1 | Forked xclip setter holds the caller's stdio pipes | tool | api-contract | none | `forked_setter.setter_return_ms: 3.3`, `serving_child.*.ppid: 1`, `serving_child.*.std_fds` are the three caller pipes, `caller_stdout_pipe_after_setter_exit: open-no-eof-for-2s`, then `caller_stdout_pipe_after_child_stopped: eof` after stopping the child by pid | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: the setter returns in ms while a re-parented serving child inherits stdin/stdout/stderr; a caller reading the setter's pipes (or a lock-holding ssh session) never sees EOF until that child dies. Spawn the server with all stdio on /dev/null and a new session, record its pid, never wait on its pipes |
| L9-2 | Detached xclip setter (stdio /dev/null, new session) | tool | api-contract | none | `detached_forked_setter_devnull: {setter_return_ms: 3.4, serving_children: 1, read_ok: true, child_session_id_differs: true}`, child stopped by pid `terminated` | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: this is the safe spawn shape for clipboard-set; the child lives on until selection is cleared or it is stopped, so ownership of its lifetime needs a stated owner |
| L9-3 | Clipboard text/image persist after the owner exits | tool | box | none (the brief's premise: ownership needs a live owner) | text: `read_after_owner_stop.equal: true`, targets then `text/plain;charset=utf-8,UTF8_STRING,TARGETS,TIMESTAMP`; PNG and 1 MB text equal too; `owner_after_race_trials.owner_process_comm: gnome-shell`; `persistence_race_trials`: 3 of 3 survived at 0, 0.05, 0.25 and 1.0 s owner lifetime | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: on GNOME X11 the shell takes over text and image selections; do not rely on it (other sessions have no such manager) and do not assert "content gone after setter exit" in tests on this box |
| L9-4 | Non-text targets need a live owner | tool | api-contract | none | `text/uri-list`, `x-special/gnome-copied-files`, `application/x-ad-u9-probe`: `read_after_owner_stop.rc: 1`, stderr `target ... not available`, `targets_after_owner_stop.rc: 1`; with owner alive `read_with_owner.equal: true` | CONFIRMS | 3.10: FileUrls (and any custom target) are only readable while a serving process lives; clipboard-set for FileUrls must leave a tracked server. Both directions measured (owner alive: present; owner stopped: absent) |
| L9-5 | One xclip serves one target | tool | api-contract | §3.10: typed `ClipboardContent::Text/Image/FileUrls` via xclip | `second_setter_replaces_first.targets_after_first: [TARGETS,UTF8_STRING]`, `targets_after_second: [TARGETS,image/png]`, `first_owner_exited_on_selection_clear: true` | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: a payload offered as several formats (text plus uri-list, gnome-copied-files plus uri-list for file managers) cannot be set with sequential xclip calls; each call replaces the previous. Needs one owner serving several targets |
| L9-6 | xclip text/image/uri/large round trip | tool | api-contract | P3-O5 "clipboard-get/set/clear via xclip (X11)" | owner-alive reads equal for `UTF8_STRING` (23 B), `image/png` (74 B), `text/uri-list`, `x-special/gnome-copied-files`, 1 MB text (INCR) ; `text_read_timing_live_owner: {min_ms: 1.91, median_ms: 2.01, max_ms: 3.34}` (process start included) | CONFIRMS | 3.10: xclip is viable for get/set on X11; read cost ~2 ms |
| L9-7 | Empty owner and no-owner reads | tool | api-contract | none | `empty_content_owner: {read_rc: 0, read_bytes: 0, targets: [TARGETS,UTF8_STRING]}`; unavailable target gives `rc: 1`, `Error: target ... not available`; `no_owner` row is contaminated by L9-3 (shell-held empty text) | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: clipboard-clear = take ownership with empty content (empty-but-successful, rc 0) vs "target not available" (rc 1) are distinct states; map the second to absent, not error |
| L9-8 | Per-window X11 capture without portal | tool | box | §3.10: "falling back to XGetImage (X11) for the lowest-permission path" | `covered.A_capture.classes: [red]` while `independent_cover_evidence.B_above_A_in_net_client_list_stacking: true` and `A_rect_inside_B_rect: true`; `uncovered` and `recovered` also `[red]`; `covered_window_returns_own_pixels: true`; no prompt | CONFIRMS | 3.10: per-window capture through the window drawable works on GNOME X11 with no permission and returns the window's own pixels when covered |
| L9-9 | Minimised window capture | tool | box | none | `minimized.A_net_wm_state_hidden: true`, `minimized.A_capture.classes: [red]` (pixbuf returned, own content) | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: a minimised window still captures on this stack; do not pre-reject minimised targets for capture |
| L9-10 | Root-window capture on composited GNOME X11 | tool | box | §3.10 / P3-O4: screenshot via `XGetImage` (X11); API Mapping: "Screenshot ... XGetImage (X11)" | `display.composited: true`, `display.window_manager: GNOME Shell`; `root_mean_abs_diff_B_shown_vs_hidden_64x40: 0.0` while a 1000x700 window was mapped then unmapped; `root_capture_stats_B_over_A.blue_cells: 0`, `red_cells: 0`; root grab returns a PNG (`root_capture.png_bytes: 411213`) | CONTRADICTS | 3.10: full-screen and `--screen` capture cannot use root XGetImage on GNOME X11 (the grab does not reflect mapped windows); compose from per-window captures or use the shell/portal path |
| L9-11 | Window and root capture cost | tool | box | none | `root_capture.timing` (1280x800 grab plus PNG encode) `{min_ms: 125.13, median_ms: 125.45, max_ms: 126.22}`; `window_capture_timing_B_1000x700` (grab only, no encode) `{min_ms: 0.92, median_ms: 0.94, max_ms: 1.29}` | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: budget; the two numbers are not like for like (encode included in root) |
| L9-12 | org.gnome.Shell.Screenshot from an unlisted client | dbus | api-contract | §3.10 lowest-permission path | `Screenshot` and `ScreenshotWindow`: `AccessDenied: ... is not allowed`, `file_exists: false`; `introspect_methods` lists Screenshot, ScreenshotWindow, ScreenshotArea, SelectArea, PickColor, FlashArea, InteractiveScreenshot | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: the shell screenshot interface is closed to arbitrary clients; do not plan on it |
| L9-13 | Portal Screenshot non-interactive, first and second call | dbus | box | P3-O11 / API Mapping: consent via XDG portal dialog | both calls: `response.code: 2`, `response.keys: []`, `after_ms: 51`, `handle_matches_predicted: true`; portal journal: `Failed to show access dialog: ... LimitsExceeded: Already showing a system access dialog`; `permission_store_after_all.screenshot_table_ids: []` | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: a host (non-sandboxed) caller goes through the shell access dialog even for `interactive:false` (the portal tried to show one); with a dialog already pending the request ends code 2 in ~50 ms with no result. The first-grant flow and "remembers a grant" are not measured (see 4) |
| L9-14 | Portal Screenshot interactive: dialog visible through AT-SPI | dbus | box | P3-O11 "portal handles consent dialog" | `interactive_1`: no Response in 8 s, `dialog_or_shell_ui_change_seen_via_atspi: false`, `gnome_shell_in_atspi_registry: false`, `root_pixels_max_mean_abs_diff_while_waiting: 0.0`; Request.Close called, `response_after_close: null`, no residual diff | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: gnome-shell is not an AT-SPI application here, so shell-drawn consent dialogs cannot be observed or answered through AT-SPI; consent detection must come from the portal Response code and Request lifecycle, not the accessibility tree |
| L9-15 | Portal availability | dbus | api-contract | P3-O11 | `portal_versions`: Screenshot 2, ScreenCast 5 (`AvailableSourceTypes: 7`, `AvailableCursorModes: 7`), RemoteDesktop 2 (`AvailableDeviceTypes: 3`), Clipboard 1; `mutter_screencast_props.Version: 4`; `org.freedesktop.impl.portal.desktop.gnome` and `.gtk` have owners | CONFIRMS | 3.10: ScreenCast and RemoteDesktop portals exist on GNOME 46; stream start and consent not exercised (no PipeWire client) |
| L9-16 | Screen Recording permission analogue | dbus | box | capability map "Screen Recording permission: PipeWire portal permission dialog" | `permission_store_before.screenshot_table_ids: []` and unchanged after; no preflight API was found; only request outcome (Response code) reports refusal | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: `PermissionReport.screen_recording` on Linux can only be inferred from a request outcome, not preflighted |
| L9-17 | Wayland: wl-clipboard, ScreenCast stream | tool | box | §3.10 Wayland path | The first probe had no Wayland session (`wayland.verdict: DEFERRED`). Measured since in a private headless shell: wl-clipboard and the Xwayland bridge in L13-10, L29-18 and L29-32; the portal Screenshot in L29-24 to L29-27; a ScreenCast frame in L29-28 | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: the clipboard and the Screenshot route are built from those rows; the portal ScreenCast stream is not built, because its `Start` raises a dialog that no test answered (L29-28), and the live-session run of it is written into 3.15 in `docs/phases.md` |

## Area L10 — shell surfaces, notifications, tray

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L10-1 | Notifications server identity | dbus | api-contract | §3.14: "detect via `GetServerInformation()`" | `notification_server.server_information.value: [gnome-shell, GNOME, 46.0, 1.2]`; capabilities `[actions, body, body-markup, icon-static, persistence, sound]`; interface exposes only `Notify, CloseNotification, GetCapabilities, GetServerInformation` plus signals `NotificationClosed, ActionInvoked, ActivationToken`. Bus owner is a unique name whose process `comm` is `gjs`, not `gnome-shell` | CONFIRMS | 3.14: detect the daemon from the `GetServerInformation` name field, never from the owner process name |
| L10-2 | `org.gnome.Shell.Notifications.GetNotifications()` | dbus | api-contract | §3.14: "GNOME: `org.gnome.Shell` exposes `org.gnome.Shell.Notifications` with `GetNotifications()`"; exit criteria "via `org.gnome.Shell.Notifications`" | the bus name `org.gnome.Shell.Notifications` is owned, but `GetNotifications_attempts` over 2 destinations x 2 paths x 2 interfaces all `read-failed`: `No such interface "org.gnome.Shell.Notifications" on object at path /org/gnome/Shell`; `Object does not exist at path /org/gnome/Shell/Notifications`; `No such method "GetNotifications"`. Recursive introspection of `/org/gnome/Shell` found interfaces `org.gnome.Shell`, `org.gnome.Shell.Extensions` and child paths `AudioDeviceSelection, Wacom, Introspect, Screenshot`; `gnome_shell_dbus.notification_like_members: []`. `Eval` not called | CONTRADICTS | 3.14: no D-Bus listing exists on GNOME 46; list from the gnome-shell AT-SPI tree (L10-3) or a `Notify` monitor |
| L10-3 | Posted notification in the gnome-shell AT-SPI tree | dbus | box | none (alternative to the list claim) | `posted_notifications.A_found_in_tree: true`, `A_seconds_to_appear: 2.24` (includes tree walk time), `A_new_node_count: 27`. Summary label, body label and a `push button` named after the action label sit under a `notification` node at depth 15-19 of the message list, all with extents `-2147483648` (list closed). A closed baseline already holds a `notification` node `[423,36,500,96]` at depth 5 and a "Clear all notifications" button at depth 11. After `CloseNotification`, `B_gone_from_tree_after_close: true` | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: list = walk the shell tree for `notification` role nodes; hidden-list nodes carry no usable extents, so index by tree order, not coordinates |
| L10-4 | Dismiss via `CloseNotification` | dbus | api-contract | §3.14: "`CloseNotification(id)` — works across all notification daemons" | for posted id `B_id`: `B_closed_signal_observed: true`, signal `NotificationClosed [id, 3]` (reason 3 = closed by call); node gone from tree. Cleanup closed ids 4, 5, 6, all `close_called: true` | CONFIRMS | 3.14: dismiss = `CloseNotification`; read reason code 3 as success. Ids come from `Notify`, so only notifications whose id was seen can be dismissed this way |
| L10-5 | Triggering `ActionInvoked` programmatically | dbus | api-contract | §3.14: "the D-Bus spec does not define a method to trigger actions programmatically — coordinate-based click ... via AT-SPI may be needed as a fallback" | introspection lists no invoke method (L10-1). In the tree the action button exists (`push button` "Probe Action") but `A_nodes_with_actions: []`: its `Action` interface answers `NActions 0`, so `DoAction` has nothing to call (`A_labelled_action_node_found: false`); extents `-2147483648`, so a coordinate click has no target while the list is closed. `ActionInvoked` was never emitted by the shell during the run | CONFIRMS | 3.14: action on a notification needs the message list opened first (so extents become real) then a coordinate click; semantic `DoAction` is not available. Whether a click emits `ActionInvoked` is unmeasured (see section 4) |
| L10-6 | AT-SPI close/dismiss control on a notification | dbus | box | none | `C_close_like_nodes: []` (no node named close/dismiss with an action under the new notification); only the list-wide `push button` "Clear all notifications" exists, `actions: []` | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: per-notification dismissal is `CloseNotification` only; "clear all" needs a coordinate click on the open list |
| L10-7 | Do Not Disturb read | tool | api-contract | §3.14: "GNOME `gsettings get org.gnome.desktop.notifications show-banners`" | `do_not_disturb.gsettings_show-banners: {rc: 0, stdout: "true"}`; `gio_settings_show_banners: true`; keys `application-children, show-banners, show-in-lock-screen`. Only the true state was read; no write was made | CONFIRMS | 3.14: DND = `show-banners == false`. The false state was not measured (read-only rule) |
| L10-8 | StatusNotifierWatcher presence | dbus | box | §3.14: "discover items via `org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher.RegisteredStatusNotifierItems`"; capability map: "AT-SPI2 `StatusNotifierWatcher` D-Bus" | `tray.org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher.name_on_bus: true`, owner `comm: gnome-shell`, `IsStatusNotifierHostRegistered: true`, `ProtocolVersion: 0`, `item_count: 2`; `org.freedesktop.StatusNotifierWatcher.name_on_bus: false`. Enabled extensions include `ubuntu-appindicators@ubuntu.com` | CONFIRMS | 3.14: probe `org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher` only. Absent-extension direction not measured (the extension was not disabled) |
| L10-9 | Item registration string format | dbus | api-contract | none | `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` entries are `:1.99@/org/ayatana/NotificationItem/software_update_available` (`raw_form: service_at_path`), i.e. `service@path`, not `service/path`; `ProtocolVersion` is 0 | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: split registered items on `@`, falling back to `/`-form and to the default path `/StatusNotifierItem` when no path is present |
| L10-10 | Item properties and menu | dbus | app | §3.14: "`Title`, `IconName`, `ToolTip`, `Menu` (D-Bus menu path) properties" | both items (interface `org.kde.StatusNotifierItem`) expose `Id, Category, Status, IconName, Title, Menu, IconThemePath, IconAccessibleDesc, AttentionIconName, AttentionAccessibleDesc, XAyatanaLabel, XAyatanaLabelGuide, XAyatanaOrderingIndex`; `ToolTip` absent (`tooltip_shape: None`). `Title: update-notifier`, `Status: Passive`. `Menu` points to a `com.canonical.dbusmenu` object: `GetLayout(0,1,[])` answered, revision 52 / 6 top-level entries and revision 2 / 4 entries, several with a null label (separators) | CONTRADICTS | 3.14: `ToolTip` must be optional; `Menu` + dbusmenu `GetLayout` is confirmed |
| L10-11 | `Activate(x, y)` and `ContextMenu(x, y)` on the item | dbus | app | §3.14: "call `Activate(x, y)` on the `StatusNotifierItem` D-Bus interface"; "call `ContextMenu(x, y)`" | introspection of both items shows interface methods only `Scroll, SecondaryActivate, XAyatanaSecondaryActivate`; no `Activate`, no `ContextMenu`. Neither was called | CONTRADICTS | 3.14: for libayatana-style items use `SecondaryActivate` or the dbusmenu; call `Activate`/`ContextMenu` only after introspection shows them. Scope is the two items on this box; items from other toolkits were not present |
| L10-12 | Passive tray items in the top panel | gi-atspi | box | none | the two `update-notifier` items appear as `menu` nodes at depth 7 of the top panel with extents `-2147483648` (Passive status hides them); a third item `AnyDesk` (`menu`, `[1136,0,40,32]`) is visible but is absent from `RegisteredStatusNotifierItems` | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: the tray listing from the watcher and from the panel tree differ; show both, and mark hidden items by Status or invalid extents |
| L10-13 | Legacy XEmbed tray | tool | box | §3.14: "older apps use the XEmbed protocol; access via the AT-SPI tree of the tray window" | `tray.xembed_selection.value: "_NET_SYSTEM_TRAY_S0:  not found."`: no X11 tray owner exists, so there is no tray window to walk. A visible legacy-style item is exposed only as a panel `menu` node (L10-12) | CONTRADICTS | 3.14: on GNOME the tray has no XEmbed window; legacy icons reachable only as nodes of the gnome-shell tree |
| L10-14 | gnome-shell AT-SPI subtree, top panel | dbus | box | P3-O12: "Dock / taskbar surface via per-DE panel process walk (GNOME Shell process...)" | `atspi.gnome_shell_accessibles: 1` (one `application` named `gnome-shell` of `registry_children: 20`); `baseline_walk: {nodes: 2006, truncated: false, seconds: 2.26}`. Top panel is a depth-3 `panel` `[0,0,1280,32]` holding `toggle button` "Activities" `[0,0,72,32]`, the system `menu` `[1176,0,104,32]` and tray `menu` nodes. Whole tree has 1115 `panel` nodes; Action interfaces are present on all, but only 1 node (a `text`) reports an action (`activate`) | CONFIRMS | 3.14: one gnome-shell app root serves panel, dock and message list; activate shell controls by coordinates since `Activities` reports `actions: []`. Walk cost above is libatspi via raw D-Bus, one run |
| L10-15 | gnome-shell AT-SPI subtree, Ubuntu dock | dbus | box | capability map: "Dock / taskbar surface: AT-SPI2 per-DE panel walk"; P3-O12 | dock is a depth-3 `panel` `[0,32,66,768]` with `push button` children named after apps (extents e.g. `[0,-1,66,60]`) plus `Trash` and a `toggle button` "Show Apps" `[0,734,66,66]`; a second copy of the app buttons sits in the overview with extents `-2147483648`. Buttons report `actions: []` | CONFIRMS | 3.14: build the dock list from the `push button` children of the left `panel`, filtering invalid extents so overview copies are dropped |
| L10-16 | Message tray / banner subtree | dbus | box | none | a banner container `panel` `[419,32,508,104]` with child `[424,37,499,94]` and a `notification` node at depth 5 `[423,36,500,96]` was present in the baseline before the probe posted anything; the probe's own notification landed in the list subtree instead (L10-3). Whether a visible banner was shown for the probe notification was not observed | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: do not assume banner nodes belong to the notification just posted; match by summary text |
| L10-17 | Extensions listing | dbus | box | none | `org.gnome.Shell.Extensions` is not a bus name (`names: false`) but `ListExtensions` on `/org/gnome/Shell` answered: `ding, tiling-assistant, ubuntu-appindicators, ubuntu-dock` | NEW-EDGE | 3.14: detect the AppIndicator extension via `ListExtensions` on `org.gnome.Shell` to build the "no tray available" suggestion |

## Area L11 — lifecycle

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L11-1 | Direct spawn pid vs app pid, cold | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "spawn via `xdg-open` or direct `Command::spawn`" | editor `cold_launch.variants[direct spawn].launcher_pid_is_app_pid: true`, `bus_name_owner_pid` equals it; calculator same (`app_found_ms` about 5) | CONFIRMS | 3.9: a direct spawn's child pid is the app pid for GApplication apps; cold first window median 343 ms editor, 325 ms calculator (`launch_latency`, min 332/289, max 349/360) |
| L11-2 | Launch by desktop id (`gio launch`, `gtk-launch`, `Gio.DesktopAppInfo.launch_uris_as_manager`) | tool | api-contract | §3.9 API Mapping: "App launch: `xdg-open` / direct process spawn: Launch by `.desktop` file or command name" | `launcher_pid_is_app_pid: false` for gio and gtk (`launcher_returncode: 0`, exit about 10 ms / 50 ms); app `app_ppid: 1` (`app_ppid_comm: systemd`); `waitpid_by_probe: ChildProcessError errno=10`; `app_cgroup` is the login session scope (no per-app scope); `api_callback_pids[0]` is in `new_pids` (`api_pid_in_new_pids: true`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: the launcher's pid is never the app's; resolve the app pid after launch (bus-name owner pid, or a pid callback from `launch_uris_as_manager`); never report the launcher pid in `LaunchResult` |
| L11-3 | `xdg-open <file>` as a launcher | tool | app | §3.9: "spawn via `xdg-open`" | editor `cold_launch.variants[xdg-open]`: `launcher_exited: false` while app alive, `app_ppid_comm: xdg-open`, `launcher_pid_is_app_pid: false`; takes a file, not an app id | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: xdg-open opens a document with the default handler and blocks while the app it spawned lives; use `gio launch`/`gtk-launch`/spawn for launching an app by id or name. Launching a `.desktop` path through xdg-open was not measured |
| L11-4 | Second launch of a single-instance app (editor) | tool | app | §3.9: "attach-vs-fail policy"; types: "`attach_if_running` true" | editor `handoff.variants[*]` (direct, gio, gtk, repeats): `launcher_exited: true`, `launcher_returncode: 0`, exit 100-130 ms (gio 10 ms), `new_app_processes_seen: []`, `new_window_pids: []`, `first_pid_windows_now: 1`; `bus_owner_after` equals `bus_owner_before` | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: for a single-instance app the attach is implicit and silent: the spawned pid exits 0 and no window appears; an attach decision must be made before spawning (bus-name owner / existing pid) and the returned pid must be the existing owner's |
| L11-5 | Second launch with args, env, cwd | tool | app | §3.9: "args/env/cwd" in `LaunchOptions` | editor `args_env_cwd.second`: `first_pid_window_titles` shows the second file opened in the first process (`any_first_pid_window_title_has_second_file: true`), but `first_pid_environ_has_second_mark: false`, `first_pid_cwd_is_second_cwd: false`, `new_window_pids: []`; `--new-window` gives `new_window_under_first_pid: true`, `new_app_processes_seen: []` | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: args reach the existing instance, env and cwd of a handed-off launch are discarded; document that `env`/`cwd` apply only when the launch creates the process (`attach_if_running` false cannot be honoured for single-instance apps) |
| L11-6 | Multi-instance app (calculator) launches | tool | app | §3.9: "attach-vs-fail policy" | calculator `handoff.variants[direct spawn]`: `launcher_exited: false`, `new_window_pids` is the new pid, `bus_owner_before: null`; `close.second_launch.second_pid_alive: true` | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: whether a second launch attaches or forks is per app; attach detection cannot assume a bus name. Calculator holds none (`close.gaction_quit.error` ServiceUnknown) |
| L11-7 | Launch failures | tool | api-contract | none | `launch_errors`: missing binary `FileNotFoundError errno=2`; `gtk-launch` rc 2 "no such application"; `gio launch` missing file rc 1; `xdg-open` missing file rc 2 (4 in a session with a bus) | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: map spawn ENOENT and gtk-launch rc 2 to `APP_NOT_FOUND`; do not treat xdg-open rc alone as launch success |
| L11-8 | SIGTERM and SIGKILL on a probe-launched child | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`SIGTERM` then `SIGKILL` under `--force`, confirmed via a follow-up liveness probe"; API Mapping: "Graceful close first" | `close.sigterm[*].returncode: -15`, `close.sigkill[*].returncode: -9`; proc state `R` then `Z` within about 7 ms (`timeline`); `window_gone_ms` about 8; gtk apps do not handle SIGTERM | CONTRADICTS | 3.9: see section 3 (SIGTERM is not a graceful step) |
| L11-9 | Liveness probe on an unreaped child | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "confirmed via a follow-up liveness probe, not assumed" | `close.sigterm[0].zombie_probe: {kill0_while_zombie: true, proc_state: Z}` | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: `kill(pid, 0)` succeeds for an exited-but-unreaped child; the liveness probe must read `/proc/<pid>/stat` and treat `Z` (or a missing entry) as exited. A non-child is gone from `/proc` about 10 ms after the signal (`cold_launch...sigterm.ms_to_gone`) |
| L11-10 | Graceful close by window close message | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "Graceful close first" | editor `close.close_last_window_wm_close`: `wmctrl -i -c` rc 0, process exits `returncode: 0` in about 216 ms (`zombie_probe` Z); with two windows `close.multi_window`: one window gone in 66 ms, `process_alive_after_one_close: true`, `remaining_windows: 1`. calculator close of its only window: exit 0 | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: graceful `close_app` = `_NET_CLOSE_WINDOW` to every window of the pid, then wait for exit; the process stays alive while other windows remain. Unsaved-document prompts were not measured |
| L11-11 | App-level quit action | dbus | app | none | editor `close.gaction_quit`: `org.gtk.Actions.List` returns 7 actions incl. `quit`; `Activate("quit")` gives `exited: true`, `returncode: 0`, 162 ms; calculator `error` ServiceUnknown | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: optional second graceful path for apps owning a bus name; not available for every app |
| L11-12 | Ungraceful X11 close helpers | tool | app | §3.9: API Mapping "Window ops: `xdotool` / `wmctrl`" | `close.xdotool_windowclose`: editor `returncode: -6` after 4082 ms (SIGABRT), calculator `returncode: 1`; `close.xdotool_windowkill`: `returncode: 1` both; rc 0 from the tool in all cases | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: never use xdotool `windowclose`/`windowkill` as a close; they destroy the connection and the app aborts or exits 1 |
| L11-13 | Exit status visibility | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "exit-code inspection -> `Exited`/`Crashed`" | child: `returncode` 0 / -15 / -9 / -6 readable; non-child (gio, gtk, API launches): `waitpid_by_probe: ChildProcessError errno=10`, process vanishes from `/proc` with no status | CONTRADICTS | 3.9: see section 3 |
| L11-14 | `/proc/<pid>/stat` after SIGSTOP | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`/proc/<pid>/stat` state character for hung detection where meaningful" | `sigstop.baseline_state: S`; `after_sigstop.state_is_T: true` in 5.2 ms (calculator) and 7.2 ms (editor), `status_state_line: State: T (stopped)`, `kill0: true` | CONFIRMS | 3.9: `T` identifies a stopped app within a few ms; an app blocked in its main loop is expected to read `S` like an idle one (not measured), so `/proc` cannot be the only signal |
| L11-15 | AT-SPI call to a stopped app | dbus | api-contract | §3.9 / phases "ProcessState": `Unresponsive` via liveness probe | calculator `sigstop.stopped_calls`: `timeout_arg_ms: -1` fails after 25025.2 ms and again 25025.2 ms (`g-io-error-quark` code 24 Timeout); explicit 1000 ms fails at 1001.2, 300 ms at 300.6; baseline `baseline_call_ms` min 0.2 / median 0.3 / max 0.8 | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: every AT-SPI read made for liveness needs an explicit short timeout; the default is 25 s. A timeout plus `/proc` state `T` maps to `Unresponsive`; a timeout alone is `TIMEOUT`; 3.5: every resolver call races the caller's deadline, and a timeout is never reported as `STALE_REF` |
| L11-16 | What still answers while the app is stopped | dbus | box | none | `registry_getchildren_while_stopped.ok: true` (0.4 ms, still lists the app); `bus_pid_query_while_stopped` pid answered in 0.2 ms; `x_server_answers_while_stopped.title` read in 2.3 ms | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: pid-from-bus-name and the X server stay responsive for a stopped app, so a pid can be resolved and `/proc` read without touching the app |
| L11-17 | Signals against a stopped app, and resume | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`SIGTERM` then `SIGKILL`" | `sigterm_to_stopped.exited_within_1500ms: false`, `status_lines` `ShdPnd: 0000000000004000`; after SIGCONT exits `-15` in about 150 ms; `sigkill_to_stopped.exited: true`; `resume.call_ok_after_cont: true`, first ok call 0.8 ms, `call_while_stopped_ok: false` | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: SIGTERM to a stopped process stays pending until SIGCONT; `close_app` on an `Unresponsive` (stopped) app must go straight to SIGKILL (or SIGCONT first) |
| L11-18 | move, resize | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`window_op` via `xdotool`/`wmctrl` for resize/move" | calculator `window_ops.ops[move]`: `effect_observed: true` at 1.0 ms, geometry exactly 140,160; `ops[resize]` 562x693 exact; editor results are clamped by the 1280x800 screen (resize to 982x802 reads y 65) | CONFIRMS | 3.9: `xdotool windowmove`/`windowsize` work and read back through `xwininfo` absolute client coordinates; GTK client-side shadow is in `_GTK_FRAME_EXTENTS` (61,61,55,67) and `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS` is absent, so frame math must use xwininfo, not extents |
| L11-19 | wmctrl `-G` geometry | tool | box | none | `wmctrl_list_row_for_window` reports x,y 280,320 for a window `xwininfo` reports at 140,160 (width/height equal) | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: do not read position from `wmctrl -l -G` (x,y come back doubled for these windows); use `xwininfo -id` |
| L11-20 | maximize / restore | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`window_op` ... maximize/restore" | calculator: `wmctrl -i -r -b add,maximized_vert,maximized_horz` sets both `_NET_WM_STATE_MAXIMIZED_*`, geometry 66,32 1214x768 (settled in about 170 ms); `remove` restores exactly 140,160 562x693; raw `_NET_WM_STATE` client message gives the same both ways | CONFIRMS | 3.9: maximize/restore via EWMH work with xdotool-free `wmctrl` or a raw client message; wait for `geom_settle_ms` before reading geometry (first read mid-transition differed) |

| L11-22 | unminimize / restore from minimized | tool | api-contract | §3.9: "`window_op` via `xdotool`/`wmctrl` for resize/move/minimize/maximize/restore" | `window_ops.unminimize_trials` (12 methods x 2 apps): `xdotool windowmap`, `windowactivate`, `windowraise`, `wmctrl -i -a`, `wmctrl -b remove,hidden`, `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` source 1/2 with timestamp 0 or a real server time, and three repeats after a 1.5 s delay: all `unhidden: false`, `wm_state_after: Iconic`, `final_hidden: true`; reproduced in three runs | CONTRADICTS | 3.9: see section 3 |

Passing direction: `calc_default.client_views`: `registry_child_count: 1`, `app_in_registry: true`, `tree.nodes: 99`; `editor_default`: `tree.nodes: 64` | NEW-EDGE | 3.1/3.4: `GetAddress` success plus an empty registry is the "app has no bus access" signature and is an answered-empty result, not an error. Cold a11y activation costs about 130 ms of start in this tree (`calc_default` min 781.8 vs 643.5), single-run comparison across private trees, not a general claim; 3.5: accepted, a ref cannot exist without a bus and the bus-absent path is unchanged |

## Area L13 — Wayland

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L13-1 | Which display variables a headless Wayland shell and the live X11 box expose | tool | box | Display Server Detection: "Check `$WAYLAND_DISPLAY` environment variable — if set, use the Wayland path"; "Check `$DISPLAY` ... if set and no Wayland, use the X11 path" | In the private shell: socket `steps.session_start.wayland_socket: "wayland-0"`, Xwayland started on `steps.private_bus.xdisplay: ":1"` (so a Wayland shell offers both). Live X11 box: `live_env: {DISPLAY: ":0", WAYLAND_DISPLAY: null, XDG_SESSION_TYPE: "tty"}`. `XDG_SESSION_TYPE` was `tty` on the X11 box, so it is not a usable signal | CONFIRMS | 3.8/3.10: keep the order WAYLAND_DISPLAY then DISPLAY; do not consult `XDG_SESSION_TYPE` |
| L13-2 | `wl-paste` with a stale and with an empty `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` | tool | api-contract | Display Server Detection: "if set, use the Wayland path" | stale name: `clipboard_wl.stale_WAYLAND_DISPLAY_paste: {rc: 1, err: "Failed to connect to a Wayland server: No such file or directory ..."}`; empty string: `empty_WAYLAND_DISPLAY_paste: {rc: 1, err: "Failed to connect to a Wayland server: Connection refused ..."}` | NEW-EDGE | 3.8/3.10: treat an empty `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` as unset, and treat "set but the socket does not connect" as a failed Wayland path that falls back to X11 only if `DISPLAY` is set; never pass the failure through as a clipboard result |
| L13-3 | Starting `gnome-shell --headless --wayland` unattended, repeatedly | tool | box | Key Risks: "Wayland a11y gaps"; 3.12 harness assumes a Wayland session can be started | `inner.shell_start_attempts` over the final three full runs: `[{stable:false, probes_ok_before_hang:0}, {stable:false, probes_ok_before_hang:1}, {stable:true, probes_ok:16}]`, `[{stable:true, probes_ok:16}]`, `[{stable:false, probes_ok_before_hang:1}, {stable:true, probes_ok:16}]`. In an unstable start the socket exists and the first `wl-paste` may even answer, then every Wayland client request (`get_registry` + `sync`) never completes while the shell process sits idle in `poll`; GTK apps launched into it never reach the a11y registry. `--nested` was not used (known to fail, per box prep) | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the Wayland tier of the harness must treat start-up as a retry loop with a health gate (a client round trip that completes repeatedly over a short window), kill and restart the shell on a failed gate, and bound the attempts; never trust "socket exists" or "`org.gnome.Shell` owned" (both were true in hung starts). Isolate the session bus (private `session.conf`) so portal/other services are not activated |
| L13-4 | Tree shape of the same GTK4 app on Wayland vs on X11 (Xwayland), raw D-Bus walk | dbus | app | Key Risks: "Wayland a11y gaps ... Focus on GNOME (best AT-SPI2 support)" | Calculator: `apps.calculator-wayland.tree.nodes: 97`, `calculator-x11.tree.nodes: 99`; `tree_compare_wayland_vs_x11.calculator.only_in_b: [["button","Minimize"],["button","Maximize"]]`, `only_in_a_count: 0` (X11 shows client-drawn window buttons, Wayland does not). Text Editor: 62 vs 66 nodes, differences are document title/state text (the document draft changed between launches) plus the same two window buttons. Same roles, depth 17-18, `read_failures: 0` on all four. Registry appearance `registry_appear_ms` 1318 / 307 (editor wayland / x11), 406 / 306 (calculator wayland / x11), one launch each | CONFIRMS | 3.4: no Wayland-specific tree handling for GTK4 on GNOME; do not key snapshot logic on window-decoration buttons being present |
| L13-5 | Roles and interfaces of the GTK4 window node under the app root | dbus | app | §3.4: "each app's top-level accessibles with role `frame`, `dialog` or `window` (GTK3 exposes only the Accessible, Component and Collection interfaces on them, with no `Window` interface; L8-2)" | In both GTK4 apps on both backends the only depth-1 node has `role: "application"` (tree role count `application: 2` = app root + window) with ifaces `["Accessible","Component","Action"]`, `has_Window_iface: false`, states `sensitive, showing, visible` (no `active`/`focused`, even after a pointer click delivered through the compositor) | CONTRADICTS | 3.4: a GTK4 window is the single child of the app root with `GetRoleName` `application` (numeric role 23, frame, per L3-2), the Accessible, Component and Action interfaces, no `Window` interface and no `ACTIVE` state in the headless Wayland session; the 3.4 sentence is corrected in place |
| L13-6 | `GetExtents` in screen (0) and window (1) coords for the window and its descendants; does Wayland report a global position | dbus | app | none (extends L6-6) | Window: `extents_before_screen == extents_before_window == [0,0,700,520]` (editor), `[0,0,360,497]` (calculator) on Wayland, and `[0,0,700,520]` / `[0,0,360,460]` under Xwayland. Descendants: screen minus window offset takes 20 / 25 / 45 / 47 distinct values (editor wayland, editor x11, calculator wayland, calculator x11) (`extents.screen_minus_window_offsets`), so screen coords are not window coords plus one frame origin. The X11 editor's real position from X was `Position: 228,59` while AT-SPI said `[0,0,...]`. No node returned `-1,-1` (`screen_minus1_minus1: 0`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.4/3.8: GTK4 gives no global position on Wayland, and none on X11 either (L6-6); coordinate input cannot be derived from AT-SPI extents for GTK4. Window-coordinate extents are the only usable geometry |
| L13-7 | `GetAccessibleAtPoint` and `Contains` at each button centre, both coord types, and at points outside the window | dbus | app | §3.6 (hit test): "`hit_test` three-way result via `Component.Contains` (geometry only, so never a negative on its own; L6-1, L6-2)" | Calculator, 12 nodes: `GetAccessibleAtPoint(ct=1)` returned the node or a descendant 12/12, `ct=0` 0/12 (returns an unnamed `generic`). `Contains` at the ct0 centre true 12/12, at the ct1 centre true 6/12 (`contains_ct1_true: 6`, `contains_false_or_error: 6`) although `GetAccessibleAtPoint` hit all 12. Editor: ct1 hit 3/9, ct0 0/9. Identical numbers under Xwayland, so not Wayland-specific. Outside the window: `negative_probes.*.at_point: ["", "/org/a11y/atspi/null"]`, `contains: false` | CONFIRMS | 3.6: `Contains` returning false does not mean "not under the point"; use `GetAccessibleAtPoint` in window coords and keep `Contains` as supporting evidence only |
| L13-8 | `DoAction` on a button with no input device present | dbus | app | Key Risks: "Prefer AT-SPI actions over coordinate input" | Calculator: seat reported "This seat has no keyboard" at that moment (`clipboard_wl_before_any_input_device`). `do_action.action0_name: {ok:"click"}`, `do_action_7: {ok:true}`, display text went `"" -> "7"` then after the second button `"78"` (`readback_changes_7`, `readback_after_8`) on Wayland and on Xwayland. Failing direction: `bad_index_99: {err: ...Unknown action 99}` | CONFIRMS | 3.7/3.8: AT-SPI actions are the dependable Wayland input; read the effect back from the app tree |
| L13-9 | `EditableText.InsertText` / `SetTextContents` in a Wayland text box with no input device | dbus | app | Key Risks: "Prefer AT-SPI actions over coordinate input" | `editable_text.insert: {ok:true}`, `readback_after_insert: "hello"`, `set_text_contents: {ok:true}`, `readback_after_set: "replaced"`, `caret_offset: {ok:8}`, text box states `focusable, sensitive, showing, visible, editable` (not focused) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: text entry through `EditableText` works on Wayland without focus or an input device |
| L13-10 | Clipboard round trip with wl-clipboard | tool | box | §3.10 / P3-O5: "`wl-clipboard` (Wayland)" | With a keyboard seat present: `roundtrip_ok: true`, `roundtrip_text.out: "ad13-clip-marker"`, `types_while_owned: "UTF8_STRING STRING TEXT text/plain;charset=utf-8 text/plain"`, `primary_roundtrip.out: "primary-marker"`; empty clipboard: `empty_clipboard_paste: {rc:1, "Nothing is copied"}` (an answer, not a hang). wl-clipboard works here with no data-control protocol: its own surface receives `wl_keyboard.enter` | CONFIRMS | 3.10: wl-copy/wl-paste usable under GNOME Wayland for text and primary selection |
| L13-11 | wl-clipboard when the seat has no keyboard | tool | box | §3.10 / P3-O5 as above | `clipboard_wl_before_any_input_device: {rc:1, message:["This seat has no keyboard"]}`; the same tool then worked once a (virtual) keyboard existed (L13-10). In a shell that had stopped serving clients the identical call hit the 6 s limit (`rc: null, error: "timeout"`, earlier runs of this probe) | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: map "no keyboard" (exit 1 with that text) and a timeout to a distinct, actionable error (headless/no-seat session), not `Nothing is copied` |
| L13-12 | Does a wl-copy selection outlive the wl-copy process | tool | box | none | `clipboard_wl.after_stop_paste: {rc:0, out:"ad13-clip-marker"}` after the `wl-copy --foreground` process group was signalled and reaped (`wl_copy_alive_during: true` before). Observed once in this capture | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: do not assume stopping the wl-copy writer clears the clipboard; `clipboard-clear` needs `wl-copy --clear`. Re-measure before relying on persistence (single observation) |

Keys via `NotifyKeyboardKeysym` reach a Wayland-native GTK4 editor: first sequence read back `keysym_readback_to_wayland_editor: "i"` (the first key `h` of `h`,`i` was dropped), second sequence `second_sequence_xyz_readback: "xyz"` (all three delivered); the dropped first key reproduced in two full runs. Window states stayed `sensitive, showing, visible` (no `active`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: a rootless Wayland key path exists in a session where the caller may use this service; lead each injected sequence with a throwaway key or wait for focus. Access control in a real user session is unmeasured (see section 4) |
| L13-17 | Pointer path: relative motion + button through the same service, aimed from AT-SPI extents | dbus | box | Key Risks: "Prefer AT-SPI actions over coordinate input" | `mutter_remote_desktop_pointer`: button extents screen `[0,0,64,44]`, window `[16,299,64,44]`; click at the AT-SPI screen centre `[32,22]` changed nothing (`click_reached_button: false`); click at the position assuming a centred window `[508,472]` changed nothing (`centred_window_hypothesis.reached: false`). `org.gnome.Shell.Introspect.GetWindows` -> `AccessDenied: GetWindows is not allowed`; `org.gnome.Shell.Screenshot.Screenshot` -> `AccessDenied` | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: raw pointer input cannot be aimed on Wayland without a global window origin, and an ordinary client cannot ask the compositor for it; keep coordinate pointer input out of the Wayland path, use AT-SPI actions |
| L13-18 | libei / EIS reachability | dbus | box | §3.8: "**`libei`** ... documented here as a researched future alternative ... not adopted" | `libei.so.1.2.1` and `libeis.so.1.2.1` installed; `Session.ConnectToEIS` returned a unix socket fd (`connect_to_eis: {fd_index:0, fd_is_socket:true}`); no EIS socket file appeared in the private runtime dir (`eis_sockets_in_private_runtime: []`). The EIS handshake was not performed | CONFIRMS | 3.8: libei is available through RemoteDesktop `ConnectToEIS`; the claim "not adopted" stands, a later phase can measure the handshake |

Reading a frame with `gst-launch-1.0 pipewiresrc path=34 ... pngenc`: `gst_capture: {rc: 255, err: "Failed to set pipeline to PAUSED."}`, `png_bytes: 0`. A 60 s limit applied (an earlier run with 25 s timed out) | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: the `PAUSED` failure was the missing session manager; with `pipewire` and `wireplumber` running the same Mutter route gave a 1280x800 frame (L29-28). | L13-22 | AT-SPI window operations on a Wayland-native window | dbus | api-contract | §3.9: window ops listed for GNOME/X11 only | `window_ops`: `set_extents_move`, `set_position`, `set_size_resize`, `set_extents_resize`, `grab_focus` all `org.freedesktop.DBus.Error.NotSupported`; extents unchanged after each (`extents_after_window` same as before); no `Window` interface (`has_Window_iface: false`) on either backend | NEW-EDGE | 3.9: window move/resize/focus through Component is not an option on GTK4, Wayland or X11 |

| L13-24 | Move / resize / minimize a Wayland-native window | dbus | box | §3.9 (as above) | No call changed a Wayland-native window: AT-SPI refused (L13-22), `GetWindows` and `Screenshot` are `AccessDenied` (L13-17), wmctrl and xdotool were not run against a Wayland-native window (it was not present at that step) | DEFERRED | needs: a compositor window-management route usable by an ordinary client (none found), plus a run of wmctrl/xdotool with a Wayland-native window mapped; closure: 3.9 |

After pushing `DISPLAY` and `XAUTHORITY` into the bus activation environment, `server_DISPLAY == my_DISPLAY` and the window existed with the active window verified (5 of 5 runs). ## Area L22 — FFI, npm and release (sub-phase 3.13)

Rows `L22-1` to `L22-15` are the pre-code probes of the sub-phase, measured on the 3.4 branch Linux crate. Rows `L22-16` onward are measured on the 3.13 branch. Probe scripts and captures stay outside the repository.

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L22-1 | Q1: ELF, dependency closure and size of the box-built CLI and cdylib | tool | box | §3.13: "minimum glibc (2.35, Ubuntu 22.04 baseline) documented" | CLI: `ELF 64-bit LSB pie, x86-64`, interpreter `/lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2`, `NEEDED` = `libgcc_s.so.1`, `libc.so.6`, no RPATH/RUNPATH, 4337576 bytes stripped. cdylib `libagent_desktop_ffi.so` (release-ffi): `NEEDED` = `libgcc_s.so.1`, `libm.so.6`, `libc.so.6`, `ld-linux-x86-64.so.2`, 4102712 bytes, no SONAME. Highest symbol versions: `GLIBC_2.34` (11-12 refs) and `GLIBC_2.39` (3 refs: `pidfd_getpid`, `pidfd_spawnp`); nothing else above 2.34. `ldd` in a clean env resolves all four libraries | NEW-EDGE | 3.13: both artifacts are far under the 15 MB cap and depend on only libgcc_s, libm and libc; no libdbus, X11 or xcb shared object is linked, so the "libdbus-1-dev" build requirement in the Release checklist has no basis (pure-Rust zbus/x11rb) |
| L22-2 | Q1: run the box-built artifacts on a real Ubuntu 22.04 glibc 2.35 userland | tool | api-contract | §3.13: "minimum glibc (2.35, Ubuntu 22.04 baseline)" | jammy `libc6 2.35-0ubuntu3.15` and `libgcc-s1 12.3.0-1ubuntu1~22.04.3` fetched over http, `InRelease` verified with `gpgv` (Ubuntu archive key), `Packages.gz` and both debs matched the index sha256, extracted unprivileged and used through `ld-linux --library-path`. CLI: `libc.so.6: version 'GLIBC_2.39' not found`, exit 1; `ld.so --list` of the cdylib resolves all libraries but loading fails with the same `GLIBC_2.39 not found`. The verneed entry is `Flags: none` (not weak), so it is a hard requirement | NEW-EDGE | 3.13: a binary built on a glibc 2.39 host cannot meet the 2.35 floor, so the Linux CLI legs of `release.yml` and `release-dry-run.yml` must build on `ubuntu-22.04` / `ubuntu-22.04-arm` like the FFI legs, and both workflows must gate on `readelf --version-info` max `GLIBC_` <= 2.35 for the CLI and the cdylib. The 2.39 references are `std` process-spawn symbols bound to the build host's libc; a 22.04-built artifact is expected to top out at 2.34 (the highest non-2.39 version above), but that was not built or run here, so the 2.35 claim stays unproven until a 22.04-hosted build is inspected |
