## Area L28 — input synthesis (sub-phase 3.8)

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L28-1 | XTEST presence and key delivery, Xvfb + openbox and private `gnome-shell --x11` (mutter), GTK 3.24, GTK 4.14, Qt 6.11 (PyQt6), each app logging its own key events | tool | api-contract | §3.8 R1/R3: XTEST keys land on a window once its frame is active | `XQueryExtension("XTEST")` true on both servers. After `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` named the target, `hello` typed through `XTestFakeKeyEvent` read back `hello` then `hellohello` (run 1, run 2) on all three toolkits under openbox; under mutter `ab` read back `ab` on all three after the target was active. Failing direction: with a second GTK3 window active, `zz` reached only that window (decoy text `zz`, 2 key events) and the target logged 0 key events and empty text (3 of 3 toolkits, both mutter runs; openbox the same) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: the `window_active` gate is the right precondition; XTEST follows the active window on X11, not the target ref |
| L28-2 | `gnome-shell --x11` private session at start-up | tool | box | §3.8 plan: Text Editor "never focused" cause is the window manager | A fresh private mutter starts with the Overview open: `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` is `0x0` after launching an app, `wmctrl -a` leaves it `0x0`, a raw click and XTEST keys reach no app (target text empty, 0 key events). One XTEST `Escape` closes the Overview and `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` becomes the newest window, after which keys land (L28-1). Seen on all three toolkits, 2 of 2 runs | NEW-EDGE | 3.8/3.12: a mutter harness must send Escape (or wait for the Overview to close) before any input check; a frame that never becomes active with no window manager decision behind it can be the shell's Overview, and the gate refusing is correct |
| L28-3 | Raw XTEST click on a window that is not active, private mutter X11; the click was also sent onto a window mapped while the Activities Overview was open (a fresh private mutter starts with it open), then after one `escape` | tool | api-contract | §3.8 U1 probe 7: does a raw click make the target active | Target at x=700, decoy (active, `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` = decoy) at x=100. `XTestFakeMotionEvent` onto the target entry then button 1 down/up: `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` became the target in 2 of 2 runs on GTK3, GTK4 and Qt, and `ab` typed next landed in the target entry (`target text ab`, decoy key count unchanged). Qt logged the press at the expected root point (900,162). Failing direction: no click, keys went to the decoy (L28-1). With the Overview open, a raw click on a never-focused window left `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` unchanged and the frame not `ACTIVE`, and the following `type` was refused and logged no key (L28-17) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: a physical click activates under mutter, so click-then-type works on X11 once the Overview is closed; a keyboard step must still re-read frame `active` after the click, never assume it. The refusal of a never-focused window under mutter comes from the open Overview, not from the window manager's focus policy |
| L28-4 | Characters outside the layout through a spare keycode (`XChangeKeyboardMapping`, XTEST, restore), Xvfb + openbox | tool | api-contract | §3.8 R2: type it by the route U1 shows works, or refuse | Spare keycode 8 (all 7 columns written with the keysym; `0x1000000+cp` for non-Latin1). `é € 日 😀 ñ` read back exactly on GTK3, GTK4 and Qt, 10 of 10 per toolkit when the restore came 50 ms after key-up (`XSync` then sleep) and in the earlier run with 0 ms and 50 ms lead delay before the press. Failing direction: restore issued immediately after key-up with no sync: GTK3 0/10, GTK4 1/10, Qt 3/10 (lost characters, never a wrong one: empty text) | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: the remap route works and need not refuse; keep the remapped keycode mapped until the event is consumed (sync, then a bounded 50 ms hold, restore after) and read the result as unverified; an immediate restore silently drops the character, so the step must not report success without that hold |
| L28-5 | Held XTEST key and button when the holder dies, Xvfb (`xinput query-state` on the XTEST keyboard and pointer) | tool | api-contract | §3.8 R5/KTD: no key or button left pressed; guard relies on the X connection | A helper held key `a` (`key[38]`) and button 1, then was killed: after `kill -9` both stayed `down` (2 of 2 trials), after `SIGTERM` (no handler) the same (2 of 2); while alive the same state showed and the app typed `a`. Failing direction: an explicit `XTestFakeKeyEvent(...,0)` and `XTestFakeButtonEvent(...,0)` from a new connection cleared both (`down: []`). The server does not release XTEST state on client disconnect | CONTRADICTS | 3.8: an in-process drop guard is the only automatic release; after a crash or `kill -9` the key stays pressed. Journal what is held (state file keyed by pid) and release stale held input at the next invocation and in `status`; never rely on connection close |
| L28-6 | Wheel clicks (button 4/5) per scroll step, list, text, tree and table widgets, Xvfb + openbox, pointer over the widget | tool | app | §3.8 wheel mapping | GTK3 and GTK4 scrolled list: 1 click = 51.2 px (`page^(2/3)` for a 366 px page; a 23 px row, so 2.2 rows), 2 = 102.3, 3 = 153.5, 5 = 255.8, identical in both trials (GTK4 first click 48.5 in trial 2). GTK3 tree and table 48.8 px per click (page 341), GTK4 list view 51.0. Qt list, tree, table: exactly 3 items per click (3, 6, 9, 15). Qt text edit 60 px, GTK text view 51.2 px per click. A step is page-size dependent on GTK, never one row | NEW-EDGE | 3.8: a wheel step cannot be converted to rows; one click moves 3 rows (Qt) or about 2 rows (GTK); `scroll --amount` must report clicks, not rows, and a one-row move needs a read-back loop. Terminal (VTE) and Chromium not measured |
| L28-7 | Which widget roles move content on wheel | tool | app | §3.8 wheel roles | List, tree, table and multi-line text moved on every toolkit measured (GTK3, GTK4, Qt; values in L28-6), 20 opposite clicks (button 4) returned it. Terminal, Chromium document and the AT-SPI roles `scroll-pane` over a non-scrollable child not measured | CONFIRMS | 3.8: allow wheel for list, tree, table, document text; refuse other roles only on measurement |
| L28-8 | Where XTEST keys go when frame active names window A but a GTK3 popup menu in app B holds the grab | tool | api-contract | L5-6 analogue, §3.8 R3 | App B opened a `Gtk.Menu` (`popped: visible true, grab true`). `wmctrl -a A` made `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` = A (true) and the menu stayed open (no deactivate). `zq` through XTEST: every key went to B's menu (`mkey` events `z z q q`), A logged 0 key events and empty text. 2 of 2 trials, A = GTK3 and A = GTK4. Frame `active` is therefore not proof that keys reach the target | CONFIRMS | 3.8: the `window_active` check is necessary and not sufficient; add a post-send read-back where available and report `verified: false` honestly; a ref whose app is not the grab holder is refused or reported when the target does not respond. L5-6 reproduced without a shell dialog |

Typing `a b c` (evdev 30, 48, 46) with no lead key read back `bc` (first key dropped, `app keys logged [b, c]`), and the app's `active` event (`True`) appeared only after that first key. Repeats failed: `CreateSession` timed out (20 s) in 5 of 5 later start-ups while other shells ran on the box (L13-3 unstable shell), so the lead-key and modifier-tap variants and the second sequence were NOT measured, and the first-key drop is a single observation here (L13-16 saw it twice with keysyms) | CONFIRMS | 3.8: the first key is lost and the seat keyboard (and the window's active state) appear only after it; send a throwaway modifier-free lead key or tap and re-read `active` before the real sequence, as L13-16 advised. ## Area L29 — capture and clipboard measurements (sub-phase 3.10)

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L29-1 | Lease descriptor inheritance after spawn, both directions | tool | box | plan U1 leg 6 / D7 | Host took flock on two files, spawned two children with `posix_spawn`. File A made inheritable: children list it in `/proc/<pid>/fd` (`/…/lease-A`), and after the host closed its copy a second process got `busy`. File B left close-on-exec (default): absent from the child's fds, second process got `acquired`. Only fds 0, 1, 2 and the deliberately inheritable memfd reached the child | CONFIRMS | 3.10: the lease fd must stay close-on-exec in the parent and be handed on by an explicit dup that clears the flag only for the one spawn; a second lease is then takeable while the owner lives only if the owner itself holds an inheritable copy, so the owner must hold the lease, not the CLI |
| L29-2 | Owner survival after kill of the host's process group; setsid vs process_group | tool | box | plan U1 leg 6 / D7 | Host run under its own session (`setsid python3`), killed with `kill -KILL -- -<host pgid>`. Child that called `setsid()` itself: ALIVE, ppid 1. Control child with no setsid in the host's group: DEAD in the same kill. Child spawned with `setpgroup=0` (own pgroup, host's session): ALIVE after the kill, but its own `setsid()` failed `EPERM` (group leader), `sid_after` stayed the host's session. Repeated in a second run: same three outcomes | CONFIRMS | 3.10: the helper calls `setsid` itself as its first act and the spawner must not use `process_group`; a failed `setsid` must be treated as an error, since the child then still shares the host's session |
| L29-3 | Finding the helper and the owner again from /proc | tool | box | plan U1 leg 6 / D7 | A library loaded by absolute path, by bare name through `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and by `./relative` path after `chdir`: `/proc/self/maps` shows the absolute resolved path in all three (`…/libdir/libadprobe.so`). `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` of a spawned owner holds its full argv and is readable by the same uid; `/proc/<pid>/exe` resolves to the interpreter (`/usr/bin/python3.12`) for a script child | CONFIRMS | 3.10: the `maps` lookup finds the helper beside the library for every load form; to re-find a running owner scan `/proc/*/cmdline` for a marker argument and check the uid, never assume `exe` names the helper for a script stand-in (a native helper would) |
| L29-4 | pidfd_open and memfd_create | tool | box | plan U1 leg 6 / D7 | `syscall(319, "ad-lease", 0)` returned fd 3 (errno 0), shown in the child as `/memfd:ad-lease (deleted)` once marked inheritable. `syscall(434, pid, 0)` returned a pidfd (errno 0); `poll` on a live child returned `[]` | CONFIRMS | 3.10: both syscalls are available here; `rustix` pidfd/memfd are usable on this kernel. The CI image was not measured |
| L29-5 | Transfer size versus the max request, INCR | tool | box | plan U1 leg 7 / D8 | Xvfb: `XMaxRequestSize` 262140 bytes, `XExtendedMaxRequestSize` 16777212 (BIG-REQUESTS present). An `application/octet-stream` owner served 1000, 200000, 262144 and 600000 bytes in one property (type unchanged, 0 chunks), and 1 MiB, 5 MiB, 20 MiB as `INCR` (2, 6, 21 chunks). Byte-for-byte equal by a raw ctypes reader and by `xclip -o` at every size | CONFIRMS | 3.10: INCR is mandatory for a reader above the single-request limit; an owner may answer in one property up to the extended limit only when BIG-REQUESTS is enabled on its connection, so a conservative owner switches to INCR above 262140 minus the request header. Test INCR at 1 MiB and above. Chunk about 1 MiB |
| L29-6 | The selection after the owner is killed, no clipboard manager (openbox) | tool | box | plan KTD / L9-3 | Owner killed, confirmed dead by `poll()`; `GetSelectionOwner` 0, `TARGETS` and a text read answered `refused`. Passing direction: while alive, owner window nonzero and read of 17 bytes succeeded. (A first run without `-quiet` was invalid: xclip forks, the killed pid was the dead parent, and the child kept serving; this is L9-1 again) | CONFIRMS | 3.10: without a manager the content dies with the owner, so the owner process must outlive the CLI (D7) |
| L29-7 | Does gnome-shell take over the selection when the owner exits (`gnome-shell --x11`, private) | tool | box | L9-3 | Owner killed after 0.7 s, two repetitions each. Taken over (new owner window `0x400004`, not the dead xclip; read equal, `xclip -o` equal, GTK3 `wait_for_text`/`wait_is_image_available` agree): `text/plain` (GTK sees `text/plain, STRING, TARGETS, TIMESTAMP`), `UTF8_STRING`, `image/png` (GTK image available true). NOT taken over, selection ownerless, every reader empty: `x-special/gnome-copied-files`, `application/octet-stream`. A 5 MiB `text/plain` was taken over but truncated: GTK read 4194304 characters, raw read not equal to the source | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: the manager keeps only text and PNG and caps text at 4 MiB, so a file copy (`x-special/gnome-copied-files`, `text/uri-list`) and custom targets still need the surviving owner process; do not rely on the shell, other sessions have no manager (L29-6); tests must not assert that text vanishes on this box. The owner itself is not identified by pid (XRes returned no id); it is the only other client of that server |
| L29-8 | GNOME Files (Nautilus 46, private session, openbox) pasting each target | tool | app | plan U1 leg 7 | Real key press `ctrl+v` after activating the window and clicking its view; destination folder empty beforehand. `x-special/gnome-copied-files` content `copy\nfile://<path>`: file appeared in the destination, twice; source kept. Same with first line `cut`: file appeared, source removed, and the clipboard owner (xclip) exited, so Files took the selection. `text/uri-list` alone (`file://…\r\n`): file appeared. `text/plain` holding a `file://` path: nothing pasted | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: Files accepts `x-special/gnome-copied-files` and also a bare `text/uri-list`; serve both for a file copy; `copy` and `cut` first lines both work; a plain-text path is not a file paste |
| L29-9 | image/png-only owner and unlisted targets | tool | box | plan U1 leg 7 | Owner offered `image/png` only: `TARGETS` answered `TARGETS, image/png` (ids decoded; first run had a 4-byte read bug in my reader, fixed), a PNG request returned the 108 bytes. A `UTF8_STRING` request to that xclip owner got a reply (no refusal) rather than `None`, which is xclip behaviour, not the protocol rule | CONFIRMS | 3.10: the stand-in advertises exactly the targets it holds; our owner must answer `None` (refuse) for any target not in `TARGETS`, and a test must not use xclip to judge refusal |
| L29-10 | Covered window on openbox: plain grab, RedirectWindow plus NameWindowPixmap | tool | box | plan U1 leg 2 / D2, D3 | `_NET_WM_CM_S0` has no owner on openbox. Red 300x300 window fully covered by a blue 500x500 one (root grab: blue at the red window's centre, checked with `xwd -root` as oracle). `XGetImage` on the covered red window directly: `000000` (not red, no error). After `XCompositeRedirectWindow(automatic)` and `XCompositeNameWindowPixmap`: pixmap pixel `ff0000` immediately, after a clear, after unmap, map and re-cover, in both runs; the root grab still blue. A window redirected before its map: pixmap `00ff00`. Naming the pixmap of an unmapped window: an X protocol error (the error code was not decoded) in both runs | CONFIRMS | 3.10: Composite redirect is the only way to grab a covered window on a manager without a compositor, and it works; ship `composite.rs`. Redirect only for the grab, then unredirect; name the pixmap after the window is mapped and map-check first (unmapped gives an X error: handle it as a non-capturable window) |
| L29-11 | Does the named pixmap hold the window's own paint, or is it blank until a repaint (real GTK3 window, covered by an override-redirect window, openbox) | tool | app | plan U1 leg 2 / D3 | Uncovered grab `00ffff`; covered direct grab `000000`; after redirect plus name, pixmap read immediately: `00ffff` in run 1 and `000000` in run 2; at +0.3 s and +1.5 s `00ffff` in both | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: a pixmap read right after `RedirectWindow` can be blank; after redirect wait for the toolkit's repaint (poll the pixmap for non-blank, bounded about 1 s, or send an expose by a no-op map-state toggle) and report `ACTION_FAILED` with a retryable suggestion, never return the blank image |
| L29-12 | Compositing overlay window grab | tool | box | plan U1 leg 2 / D2 | Openbox (no compositor): `XCompositeGetOverlayWindow` returned a window (id 63) without error; `GetImage` on it equals the root grab (blue where windows are, `000000` elsewhere), so it adds nothing. Under `gnome-shell --x11` (own owner of `_NET_WM_CM_S0`): see L29-13 | CONFIRMS | 3.10: no use for the overlay grab on a non-compositing manager; do not take the overlay window at all there (it must be released if taken) |
| L29-13 | Root and overlay grab under gnome-shell --x11, with application windows present | tool | box | plan U1 leg 4 / D5 | Override-redirect painters drawn with `FillRectangle` (red, blue), and separately a real GTK3 window at known coordinates. Root and overlay grabs were identical to each other and contained only the shell's stage (background gradient `222222`/`023c88`, top bar `131313`); at the painters' and the GTK window's coordinates the grab was `222222` or `023c88`, not `ff0000`, `0000ff` or `00ffff`, in repeated samples over 8 s and in 2 of 2 runs per variant. `xwd -root` matched. Per-window redirected pixmaps were correct: red `ff0000`, blue `0000ff`, GTK window `00ffff`, and red stayed red in the region another window covers. The shell's own Screenshot D-Bus method answered `AccessDenied` (no oracle from there) | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: under a compositing manager the root/overlay grab does not show the applications on this box, so `screenshot` full-screen on X11 must either compose from per-window redirected pixmaps in `_NET_CLIENT_LIST_STACKING` order, or answer `PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED`; a root grab under a compositor must not be returned as the screen. Scope: Xvfb with software rendering; a hardware X session was not measured |
| L29-14 | The screen area no X window owns | tool | box | plan U1 leg 4 / D5 | Under openbox: the area outside windows is the root's own pixels (`000000`), grab is honest. Under gnome-shell: the wallpaper, the top bar and every shell-drawn surface (overview, popups, notifications) belong to the stage, not to an X window; no X call returns them truthfully (L29-13). Unowned area under the compositor therefore cannot be stated | NEW-EDGE | 3.10: a composed full-screen image under gnome-shell would have unknown pixels in the unowned area (shell chrome, wallpaper); do not silently fill with black. Recommend: full-screen under a compositor is refused with `PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED` naming the cause, per-window capture stays supported |
| L29-15 | Window unit on openbox | dbus + tool | app | plan KTD4 | GTK3: AT-SPI `[489,236,302,327]`; client `[490,258,300,300]` (ratios 0.0066 w, 0.0826 h: FAILS); frame `[489,236,302,327]` (0.0, 0.0: passes), extents `1,1,22,5`. GTK4 Files: AT-SPI `[0,0,890,550]` (no global origin); client `890x550` and its parent frame `890x550` with extents `0,0,0,0` (client-side decoration); both 0.0. Timestamp 0 (source 2): 6 of 6 GTK3 and 6 of 6 Qt took effect (plus 8 of 8 in later sequences). Title-band click: 0 of 6 GTK3 and 0 of 6 Qt (the window stayed inactive; 0 of 4 more in earlier sequences). Client-area click: 6 of 6 GTK3 and 6 of 6 Qt. A refused window carried `_NET_WM_STATE_DEMANDS_ATTENTION` in only some cases (1 of 6 GTK3 band, 0 of 6 Qt), so it is no reliable signal. | L31-59 | Insider E2E of the frozen release binary, stopped apps: `move-window`, `resize-window`, `minimize`, `maximize`, `restore`, `press`, `focus-window` by `--window-id` (taken before the stop), `--title` and `--app` on GTK3, GTK4, Qt, Calculator and Files under openbox and mutter, two sessions each | tool | api-contract | §3.9 U3 and U5: a stopped app answers `APP_UNRESPONSIVE` on every path | The frozen binary answered `WINDOW_NOT_FOUND` for every window path and `frame_not_active` for `press`: the synthesised window had a new id, so an id taken before the stop never matched, and `press` checked the frame before the process state. After the fixes, the release binary answers `APP_UNRESPONSIVE`, `not_delivered`, in 10 to 70 ms for all ten commands on all five targets (GTK3, GTK4, Qt 6 stubs, Calculator, Files) | NEW-EDGE | fixed: window ids are remembered per X11 client and process instance in a private runtime directory and reused for the stopped app's window (`window_ids.rs`, `a_window_listed_before_the_stop_keeps_the_id_it_had`); `press` checks the stopped state in the key gate (`a_stopped_target_is_unresponsive_and_nothing_is_synthesised`); both seen to fail without the fix |

After: a resize leaves the client at 300,200 on both toolkits, a size that fits is read again after 120 ms, and the GTK4 request answers `ACTION_FAILED`, `delivered_unverified`, with the held size 1280x800 and the place given back; GTK3 really takes 5000x5000 and says `ok` | NEW-EDGE | fixed with `a_resize_that_shifts_the_client_gives_the_place_back`, `a_size_that_fits_only_for_a_moment_is_not_a_success` and `a_clamped_resize_that_also_shifted_the_client_still_gives_the_place_back`, each seen to fail without the fix; checked live with `xwininfo` |

Now any open dialog or transient top-level stops the request: `ACTION_FAILED`, cause `dialog_pending`, `delivered_unverified`, `dialogs` and `windows_remaining` in `details`, nothing sent; the dialog count is unchanged after the call | NEW-EDGE | fixed with `an_open_dialog_is_reported_and_no_close_request_is_sent_to_anything` and the live unsaved-document case, which fails without the check |

Now an unanswered stamped request is resent after 400 ms (a display that already names the window is still waited on for the whole grace): the resend case takes under 1.5 s in the unit test and a granted request still takes about 80 ms. A zenity dialog: `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` and the X input focus name the dialog while its AT-SPI frame carries `MODAL` and no `ACTIVE` or `FOCUSED`, so the rule that the application confirms the focus refused it with `frame_not_active`. After the fixes: the first close answers `ACTION_FAILED` `dialog_pending` `delivered_unverified` in about 115 ms and names "Save Changes?"; the repeated close is `not_delivered`; the unknown application is `not_delivered` and safe; the same window lists `visible: false` on desktop 0 and `true` once desktop 1 is shown. `visible` on another Space is false on macOS (`kCGWindowIsOnscreen`) and a window on another virtual desktop is cloaked and left out of the inventory on Windows | NEW-EDGE | fixed with `a_dialog_that_opens_after_the_request_ends_the_wait_at_once_with_its_title`, `an_open_dialog_is_reported_and_no_close_request_is_sent_to_anything`, `an_unknown_target_is_refused_before_anything_is_sent`, `a_window_on_another_desktop_is_not_visible` and the live close case; the disposition of rejections that core builds, the unnamed target of unscoped window commands, `close-app` picking an instance and negative numbers are on the core list in `docs/phases.md` |

After the fixes: the no-window launch answers in about 2 s (15 s before), a GTK3 oversize resize answers `ok` at 5000x5000 with `xwininfo` agreeing, a forwarded launch waits up to 5 s for a window absent before the spawn and otherwise answers with no window, and the close says `wayland_session`. The keys of `press --window-id` to a second window go to the active window of the process because the adapter receives the process, not the window | NEW-EDGE | fixed with `live_a_launch_that_shows_no_window_does_not_wait_for_the_whole_timeout` (15.0 s without the fix), `a_resize_beyond_every_display_is_not_judged_by_the_place_the_manager_gave_it` and `a_wayland_session_with_no_xwayland_is_not_supported_with_its_cause`, each seen to fail without the fix; fixed in core (owner-approved, `fix(core)` commit): a headless `press --window-id` naming another window of the active process is refused before the adapter is called, `press_window_tests` cases fail without the check, and live a GTK3 process with two windows refuses `press y --window-id w2` while w1 is active (no key in either window) and `--headed` lands `y` in w2; after an oversize resize the app is busy repainting and `list-windows` omitted it for a while, accepted as the toolkit's |

## Area L33 — signals and wait parity (sub-phase 3.11)

Private sessions with their own session and AT-SPI buses, Xvfb and Openbox; raw AT-SPI over Gio as the tree oracle, `xdotool` for keys, the release binary and release test binaries for timings. Rows are the pre-code probes of the sub-phase followed by the measurements of its four build lanes and the live legs. The box ran other sessions at the same time, so every latency is an upper bound.

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L33-1 | App-root children at idle (U1 item 1): Calculator, Files, Text Editor (GTK 4), Terminal with menu bar and with header bar, PyGObject window (GTK 3), Qt window, Chrome, VS Code | dbus | app | role-39 root children are 0 at idle on GTK 3, Qt, Chromium (KTD8) | Role-39 root children: 0 on all eight. Root children at idle: GTK 4 one window-shaped child (numeric role `application`, `ACTIVE SHOWING VISIBLE`, Component+Action, no `class`); GTK 3 one `frame`; Qt one `frame` (path counter 2147483649); Chrome THREE `frame` children (`BrowserRootView` titled, plus two nameless `RootView`, all `SHOWING`); VS Code one `frame` (`RootView`, titled) | CONFIRMS | 3.11 U3: the `SHOWING` clause on the role-39 branch is not needed for idle; the two nameless `RootView` Chrome frames at idle are the existing `is_bubble` case |
| L33-2 | GTK 4 popover and dialogs at the app root (U1 items 1, 4): Calculator `Main Menu` inner toggle (`click`), then the last menu item (6 showing items), 2 cycles; Text Editor `Main menu` then the last of 11 items, 2 cycles | dbus | app | popover is never an app-root child (L18-1); About opener adds a root child (L18-12) | Opening `Main Menu` leaves the root child list unchanged at 0.05, 0.4 and 1.5 s (1 child). The last item adds a root child `application`/`About` `ACTIVE MODAL SHOWING VISIBLE`, 1 child, path a fresh uuid each cycle; the main window loses `ACTIVE`; Escape removes it (child list back to 1 within 0.5 s, 2 of 2). Text Editor last item: no root child; a role-16 `dialog` node, empty name, `SENSITIVE SHOWING VISIBLE` (no `MODAL`, no `ACTIVE`), depth 6 under the window (application > application > generic x4), first seen by a whole-tree walk 0.675 and 1.488 s after the call (127 nodes before, 137 after); Escape removed it (no role-16 node afterwards, 2 of 2). `Close`/`OK` buttons do not exist in it | CONFIRMS (refines L18-14) | 3.11 U8: the in-window dialog is a depth-6 role-16 node, gone by node absence after Escape (L18-14 saw it stay after a `Close` control that this probe did not find); it needs a bounded subtree scan; whether to build it stays the U8 decision |
| L33-3 | GTK 3 header-bar popover container (U1 item 1, Rule B): Terminal `Menu` toggle (`click`), 2 cycles | dbus | app | the header popover's `MODAL` role-39 container is an app-root child, or not (decides Rule B) | The `MODAL` role-39 panel is at depth 2 (application > frame > panel), NOT an app-root child, present while closed (`ENABLED MODAL SENSITIVE`, no `SHOWING`) and open (`SHOWING VISIBLE` added); root children stay 1 (`frame`, 3 children) at 0.05 s and 0.5 s after open and after close | CONFIRMS | 3.11 U6 Rule B stays: nothing in the window shape admits it, so opening the popover is not `window-opened`; the detector needs the toggle `CHECKED` + container `SHOWING` conjunct |

| L33-5 | Chromium menu frame (U1 item 3): Chrome `alt+f` then Escape, 2 cycles (the first `alt+f` of a session lost focus once and opened nothing; a retry opened it) | dbus | app | does the menu frame pass `is_bubble` (class `RootView`, blank name) | A fourth root child appears 0.05 s after the key: role `frame`, name blank, `ACTIVE SHOWING VISIBLE`, class `MenuHostRootView` (not `RootView`), 1 child. `is_bubble` compares the class with `RootView` exactly, so this frame is NOT excluded: it is listed as a window and takes `ACTIVE` from the browser frame. Escape removes it (root children back to 3 within 0.5 s, 2 of 2) | CONTRADICTS (the frame is not a bubble) | 3.11 U3: the window predicate must exclude `MenuHostRootView` (a `class` test on the frame, not a walked tree); without it every Chrome menu is `window-opened` and a focus candidate |

The blocked PyGObject app (L33-7) missed the slice with state not `T`, and the pass returned ok | CONFIRMS | 3.11 U4/U5: `stopped_target` (state `T`) is the only error source; a flapping stop is reported when a read lands inside a stop and is not an error otherwise; no Electron slow case was reproducible |

## Area L32 — menus and shell surfaces (sub-phase 3.14)

Pre-code probes for menus first, then notifications (L32-1 to L32-16), then the lane and integration runs of the built branch (L32-17 onward). Method: private sessions (own session and AT-SPI buses, own Xvfb, Openbox for the menu rows, `gnome-shell --x11` on the session Xvfb for the shell rows), raw AT-SPI over Gio as the tree oracle, `xwininfo`, `xprop`, `xdotool`, a screen read and a second D-Bus client as the window-system and signal oracles.

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |

Root states `SENSITIVE SHOWING VERTICAL VISIBLE` (no `ACTIVE`). The subtree is created on open (40, 63, 60, 32, 31 new nodes) and destroyed on close (0 of N nodes present after close; node count back to the baseline in 9 of 10 runs; one Files run differed by page churn, not menu nodes). The object path is reused on re-open for Calculator and Text Editor main menus and differs for Files context menus | CONFIRMS | 3.14 U14: GTK 4 popups are read from the window walk; the closed state is "no menu nodes", not "menu nodes with SHOWING false" (L32-9). Detect by new `menu` role 33 subtree with `SHOWING` under the frame |

| L32-17 | Tray corner cases and virtual tray node fit against core's identity and actionability contracts (plan U1 items 7 and 11), measured with the built adapter and a private StatusNotifierWatcher plus KDE-style and Ayatana-style receivers | dbus | api-contract | plan U1 item 7: virtual menu children may not satisfy core's identity or actionability contract; L32-16 left tray corner cases open | Identity: `IdentifierKind` has no menu variant, so a tray item is persisted with value `tray:v1:item\|owner\|path` and a menu entry with `tray:v1:entry\|owner\|path\|menu\|id\|label/path`, under the typed kind `RuntimeId` (kind `Unknown` is dropped by core's ref validation, L32-34). Roles `menubutton`, `menuitem` and `separator` are canonical, an item and an enabled entry are ref-able, a disabled entry and a separator advertise no `Click`. The layout revision is not part of the identity: it moves on any change in the menu (the checkable toggle moved it 1 to 2), and an identity that carried it would stale every ref after an unrelated change. Resolution walks the label path, so a repeated label at one level is `AMBIGUOUS_TARGET`, a missing label or a label now carrying another id is `STALE_REF`. Actionability, read from core `gates.rs` and confirmed through the CLI against a real shell (L32-36): a node with no bounds blocks `visibility` as unknown, so a bound item reports the extents of the shell panel node whose title is unique on both sides, and a menu entry reports its item's panel extents with the offscreen state unknown; an unpaired item and the entries under it report no bounds and offscreen true, which core refuses. Headed mode reaches the pointer path through a tray branch in `hit_test` (reaches when the point is inside the panel node's extents on a display, otherwise unknown). Live (6 receiver-log tests, oracle: the receiver's own log): listing parses all three registration forms and drops an exited item; `Passive` comes from `Status` while the node keeps valid extents; a headless `Click` on an item listing `Activate` is delivered (log `Activate 0 0`), a right click with `ContextMenu` likewise; an item listing only `SecondaryActivate` and `XAyatanaSecondaryActivate` is refused `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED` with `physical_delivery_started: false`, the log gains no line and `Secondary` appears 0 times; an enabled entry delivers `Event(id,"clicked")`, a disabled entry and a separator are refused with no event, a checkable pick is verified by `toggle-state` flipping, a submenu parent sends `AboutToShow` and no click; an item restarted under the same well-known name is a `STALE_REF` for the old ref because the ref binds the unique name (binding the well-known name made the restart test fail). Not measured: `ItemIsMenu` and `NeedsAttention` in the real shell | NEW-EDGE | 3.14 U8 and U9: persist the unique owner, never a well-known name; report real panel extents or none, with offscreen unknown for an entry under a shown item and true when unobserved; the headed pointer route has its tray branch in `hit_test` |

Stated limit: a Wayland session without PipeWire cannot click inside a popup. Two virtual monitors (`--virtual-monitor` twice: Meta-0 1280x800 at 0,0 primary and Meta-1 1024x768 at 1280,0) are accepted. The stream of Meta-1 maps correctly: in 5 sessions a GTK client fullscreen on Meta-1 received `enter` at exactly the monitor-local point sent (501,301; 200,150; 300,300; 501,301; 200,150), and in the 2 warm cases motion, press and release arrived at the same coordinates plus one pixel (201,151 and 401,401); a client on Meta-0 received `motion 101,201` and `press 101,201` for 100,200 on the Meta-0 stream. In the cold first session after a shell start the press that follows the first move onto Meta-1 was lost in 3 of 3 runs, whether the sequence was the adapter's (move, 150 ms, press) or a slower one, while the shell's own controls on Meta-0 took their first press in every command-line run, so the loss is the receiving client's pointer focus and not the connector or the coordinate space; no shell control can be reached on Meta-1 to test the adapter path itself. DisplayConfig did not answer in some starts of the two-monitor shell, so the start also gates on `GetCurrentState`. The headless virtual monitor offers supported scales `[1.0]` only, so a scale-2 configuration cannot be made there |
| L32-47 | Lost accessibility connection on an owned real D-Bus daemon, with a live registry protocol fixture and a stopped Calculator control | dbus + tool | api-contract | Decision 42: post-connect loss has cold-start recovery and honest retry semantics | The fixture first serves a role and empty child list, then a method refusal; the refusal is `INTERNAL`, not bus loss. Killing and waiting for the owned daemon is the independent exit oracle. The retained connection returns `PLATFORM_NOT_SUPPORTED` twice with the cold-start recovery suggestion, including caller/adapter recreation, `retryable: false`, and unknown delivery. Node reads cannot return a partial tree after loss. The required shell-role read also preserves transport failure, so loss after a successful children reply cannot masquerade as a destroyed sibling; a real missing object still returns no role. A separately stopped Calculator is confirmed by `/proc` and returns `APP_UNRESPONSIVE`, `not_delivered`, while the registry still answers | NEW-EDGE | Fixed here: transport errors survive field projection and classify like an unreachable accessibility bus, without inventing delivery evidence. Live tests `bus_live_loss_keeps_cold_start_recovery_without_claiming_delivery`, `bus_live_stopped_app_is_unresponsive_while_the_bus_still_answers` and `shell_live_lost_role_connection_is_not_a_destroyed_node`; mutations and byte-exact restoration in `probes/linux/shell-hardening-inverts.py` |
| L32-48 | Shell outage after raw-window readiness: 200 samples per run at nominal 200 ms, actual timestamps retained, two private X11 shells and two private headless Wayland shells; notification posts, message-list toggles, raw D-Bus and libatspi before/after cache clearing | dbus + gi-atspi | box | L32-45: determine whether the adapter loses a live shell window | All four runs had exactly two samples where direct reads of the still-registered shell root and its last healthy address timed out at the 2 s call limit. Every successful raw read found a role-69 content window. PID and `/proc` start token stayed unchanged, process states were R/S, and the message-list control was observed showing in 89 samples per run. Libatspi read an empty name and child count -1 during stalls; clearing its cache did not make the initial stalled read answer. A field-level follow-up (one more run per tier, 200 samples each) issued Name and GetChildren concurrently: both timed out together in exactly two samples per run, with no name-only or children-only failure; one registered shell root remained at :1.0. This is an unanswering shell service, not proof of an empty child list. The old adapter discarded the read error and reported a missing window. A real D-Bus fixture now acknowledges entry into an abortable wait gate: its read deadline remains `TIMEOUT`; a successful empty child list remains absent and never substitutes the app as a window | NEW-EDGE | Fixed here: `content_window` and `find_root` preserve read errors and skip only a destroyed sibling, rather than fabricating `window_missing`. Live owner: `bus_live_loss_keeps_cold_start_recovery_without_claiming_delivery`; healthy actual-shell owner: `shell_live_window_matches_direct_bus_reads_across_notification_posts`, passing on X11 and Wayland. Accepted limit: the adapter cannot read an unanswering GNOME AT-SPI service; the internal shell reason for the stall is not isolated. It returns the read failure and never reconstructs a shell window from unrelated evidence. Reproducer: `probes/linux/shell-outage.py` |
| L32-49 | Owned private shell SIGSTOP/SIGCONT on X11 and Wayland, independent session-bus owner PID, runtime ownership and `/proc` stopped-state checks | dbus + tool | api-contract | A really stopped process is `APP_UNRESPONSIVE`, not a transient missing window | Both tiers first matched the adapter root against a separate raw D-Bus connection. After SIGSTOP, `/proc` confirmed T and `find_root` returned `APP_UNRESPONSIVE` with `not_delivered`; after SIGCONT, the direct window and adapter root matched again. Three independently sent notifications also preserved the raw/adapter window identity in both tiers | NEW-EDGE | Fixed here: the shell resolver checks the verified shell process's stopped state before reading its tree. Tests `shell_live_stopped_process_is_unresponsive_instead_of_a_window_outage` and `shell_live_window_matches_direct_bus_reads_across_notification_posts`; the inverse removes the stopped guard or substitutes the application root for the window |

Before the change `expand` on the label popover answered `ACTION_FAILED`, kind `popup_not_observed`, `delivered_unverified`, with `popup_present` false and `postcondition_satisfied` true, after 2.1 s on the fixture and 2.7 s on Settings in Openbox and after 2.0 s and 3.0 s in Mutter, while the raw `expanded` state, the label node and the fixture log showed the popover open; a second `expand` on the open button answered `ACTION_FAILED`, `popup_not_observed`, `not_delivered`, after 1.7 s and 2.4 s in Openbox and 1.7 s and 2.3 s in Mutter, and `collapse` and its repeat were `delivered_verified` and a no-op; the button popover and the menu popover verified in 0.5 s to 0.7 s. After the change the label popover answers `expand` in 0.5 s `delivered_verified`, a second `expand` in 0.2 s `ok` with `not_delivered` and a skipped step, `collapse` in 0.6 s `delivered_verified` and a second `collapse` `ok` as a no-op on the fixture in Openbox, and the same four calls in 0.9 s, 0.5 s, 0.8 s and 0.5 s on Settings; in Mutter the fixture answers in 0.5 s, 0.2 s, 0.4 s and 0.2 s and Settings in 1.2 s, 0.5 s, 0.7 s and 0.5 s; the two buttons that open nothing still answer `popup_not_observed` after 3.2 s | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the popup rule accepted only a popover that holds a showing button, so a label popover was never a popup; it is now the one showing panel child of an expanded button whose inner toggle is pressed, and the menu surface listing is unchanged |
| L40-2 | Selection indices of a GTK4 `GtkListBox`: an own fixture with a plain list of five rows and lists with hidden rows, and the Settings category list, `IsChildSelected` read for every index until it was refused, `SelectChild` for every index, the fixture's own `row-selected` log and the raw `selected` states as oracles, read against the GTK 4.14.5 source | gi-atspi | app | §3.7 Select: GTK4 indexed list selection is refused because accessible children filter non-presented widgets while `SelectChild` can index native rows | The plain list has 5 presented rows and 5 native rows, and `SelectChild(k)` selects row `k`. A list of 7 native rows with 2 hidden has 5 presented children: `SelectChild(2)` selected the hidden row, no presented row read `selected`, `SelectChild(3)` selected the presented third row. The Settings category list has 23 accessible children, 19 rows and 4 separators listed after them, and 27 native rows; the selected row has native index 5 and presented rank 0; `SelectChild(3)` left `NSelectedChildren` 1 with no readable selected child, `SelectChild(8)` selected "Displays", and the page did not change either time. The source confirms it: `SelectChild` and `IsChildSelected` of a list box take `gtk_list_box_get_row_at_index`, the accessible child list skips what `gtk_accessible_should_present` refuses | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the refusal stands for list boxes, and a selection write that cannot be proven moves the selection to a hidden row, so no headless route exists for such a list |
| L40-3 | `select` through the release CLI on a GTK4 list box, headless and `--headed`, before and after the change, on the own fixture (a plain list, a list with hidden rows) and on the Settings category list, with the fixture's own `row-selected` log, the raw `selected` states and the page labels as oracles | gi-atspi + tool | app | L40-2: a headless select has no safe route; the documented way is a headed click on the row | Before the change every `select` that matched a row, headless or `--headed`, answered `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`, kind `selection_index_unproven`, `not_delivered`, in 0.2 s to 0.8 s, and nothing changed (the fixture logs read `Alpha` and `One`, Settings stayed on Network). After the change a headless `select` answers the same refusal in 0.1 s on the plain list, 0.1 s on the list with hidden rows and 0.3 s on Settings, and nothing changes. `--headed select` clicks the row: plain list "Gamma" in 0.5 s `delivered_verified`, with the steps `Selection.SelectChild` skipped and `XTEST.click` succeeded and verified, and the fixture log reads Gamma; the same call again 0.1 s `ok` with `not_delivered` and both steps skipped; the list with hidden rows "Three" in 0.2 s, log Three; "Ghost1", the hidden row, `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND` and the log still Three; an absent name `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND`. On Settings "Sound" answers `delivered_verified` in 0.9 s, the row reads `selected` and the page labels change to the Sound page; again `ok` with `not_delivered` in 0.5 s; "No Such Panel" `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND`; "Network" `delivered_verified` in 0.9 s and the Network page comes back | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: a headed select clicks the matched row after the refusal and judges by the row's `selected` state, a headless select keeps the refusal |
| L40-4 | The libadwaita combo row of Settings Power "Screen Blank" and of an own libadwaita 1.5 fixture, closed and after a headed click, raw AT-SPI tree, actions, states and `IsChildSelected` of the lists | gi-atspi + tool | app | L14-10: libadwaita combo rows expose no choices and no action | Closed, the combo box has states `focusable`, `selectable`, `sensitive`, `showing` and `visible`, no `has-popup`, no action and one child; below it a title panel with two labels and a list with `Selection` and one item whose label is the current value. After a headed click a showing panel with the action `default.activate` appears 4 levels below the row and holds a scroll pane and a second `Selection` list, 8 levels below the row, with one item per choice (4 in the fixture, 10 in Settings), the selected choice read by `GetSelectedChild`; the first choice label sits 11 levels below the combo, so a search from the combo stops at its depth limit of 8 while a search from the popup list finds them. Without a pointer the choices are absent, as L14-10 measured | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the choices of the row are enumerable once a pointer has opened it, and the popup commits through its list and its default action |
| L40-5 | `select` through the release CLI on the libadwaita combo row, before and after the change, headless and `--headed`, on the Settings Power "Screen Blank" row and on the own fixture, with the fixture's own `notify::selected` log, the first label of the row's current list, the number of selection lists below the row and `gsettings` `idle-delay` as oracles | gi-atspi + tool | app | L40-4: a headless select cannot open the row, and its choices appear after a pointer click | Before the change the row advertised no `Select` and `select` answered `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`, "Target is not actionable: supported_action (Select or Click is not available)", `not_delivered`, in 0.2 s for a valid choice and 0.2 s for an absent one on Settings. After the change the snapshot gives the row `Select`. Headless `select` answers `POLICY_DENIED`, kind `pointer_required`, `not_delivered`, in 0.1 s on the fixture and 0.2 s on Settings, and the value stays (fixture log `5 minutes`, `idle-delay` 300). `--headed select` "Never" answers `delivered_verified` in 0.4 s on the fixture and 0.5 s on Settings with the steps `XTEST.click` (physical) and `Action.DoAction` (semantic, verified): the row shows Never, the fixture log reads Never, `idle-delay` reads 0, and one selection list is left below the row, so the popup had closed. The same call again answers `ok` with `not_delivered` in 0.1 s and a skipped click. "NoSuchChoice" answers `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND` in 0.3 s on the fixture and 0.4 s on Settings, the popup is closed, and the value stays; "1 minute" on the fixture and "5 minutes" on Settings (`idle-delay` back to 300) answer `delivered_verified` in 0.6 s and 0.4 s. A first build that clicked the choice in the popup answered `STALE_REF`, kind `pointer_target_not_visible`, for every choice that needed a click, because the placement of the popup window is not known to the pointer check, while the click on the combo itself opened the popup each time | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: a headed select opens the row with the pointer and commits through the list of the popup and its default action, because a pointer click on a choice of the popup is refused |
| L40-6 | The popup list of the libadwaita fixture combo opened by a headed click: `IsChildSelected` for every index, `SelectChild`, the default action of the popover, read against the GTK 4.14.5 list view source | gi-atspi | app | L30-37: the popup list is committed by `SelectChild` and the popover default action | The list of 4 choices answered `IsChildSelected` for 0 to 3 (`[false, false, true, false]`) and refused index 4 with `InvalidArgs` "No child". `SelectChild(3)` answered true and moved only the selection of the popup list (the combo still showed the old value and the fixture log did not change); the default action of the popover then set the value "Never", the log read Never and the popup closed. The source shows why the index is safe here: a list view takes `SelectChild` and `IsChildSelected` as the index among its item widgets, resolves the model position of that widget itself and counts the same widgets that the accessible child list presents unless one is not presented. A list box with hidden rows fails the same count (L40-2: 7 against 5, Settings 27 against 23) | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the index of an open popup list is proven by the call about the last presented child answering and the call about the next index refusing, and no other list takes the counted write |
| L40-7 | `select` through the release CLI on a GTK4 drop down (a `Gtk.DropDown` of "Alpha", "Beta" and "Gamma" in the window of the combo row fixture), headless and `--headed`, before and after the change, with the fixture's own `notify::selected` log as oracle | gi-atspi + tool | app | L30-37: the popup list of a drop down is committed by `SelectChild` and the popover default action; L40-6: the index of that list is provable | Before the change a headless `select` of "Gamma" answered `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`, kind `selection_index_unproven`, `not_delivered`, in 0.8 s, the same call again answered the same in 0.7 s, "Nothing" answered `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND` in 0.6 s and `--headed select` of "Beta" answered `selection_index_unproven` in 0.7 s; the fixture log never moved. After the change a headless `select` of "Gamma" answers `delivered_verified` in 0.5 s with one step, `Action.DoAction` (semantic, verified), and the fixture log reads Gamma; the same call again answers `ok` with `not_delivered` and a skipped verified step in 0.1 s; "Nothing" answers `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND` in 0.7 s and the log stays Gamma; `--headed select` of "Beta" answers `delivered_verified` in 0.6 s and the log reads Beta | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: the counted index proof lets a drop down select without a pointer, and a drop down that already shows the wanted value is a no-op |
| L40-8 | `select` through the release CLI on a GTK4 list box (a plain list and one with hidden rows), the libadwaita combo row and a GTK4 drop down, headless and `--headed`, in a private Wayland shell session on the own fixtures, before and after core refuses a headed select in a session without pointer input, with the fixtures' own `row-selected` and `notify::selected` logs and the number of selection lists below the combo row as oracles | gi-atspi + tool | app | L40-3 and L40-5: the pointer routes need X11, and the CLI's Wayland pointer gate refuses pointer commands at once | Before the change every headed `select` on Wayland answered `TIMEOUT`, kind `actionability_timeout`, `not_delivered`, after 5.0 s, for the list row, the hidden-rows list, the combo row (a valid and an absent choice) and the drop down, and nothing changed. After the change each answers `ACTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`, cause `wayland_session`, `not_delivered`, in 6 ms, before any target is resolved; the logs keep their values (Alpha, One, 5 minutes, Gamma) and no popup list is added below the combo row. Headless `select` on Wayland is unchanged: the list answers `selection_index_unproven` in 0.1 s, the combo row `POLICY_DENIED`, kind `pointer_required`, in 0.1 s, and the drop down `delivered_verified` in 0.4 s through the semantic route. On X11 the same headed calls still deliver (list "Gamma" and "Three" and combo row "Never" `delivered_verified`, "NoSuchChoice" `ELEMENT_NOT_FOUND`) | NEW-EDGE | 3.12: core asks the adapter whether the session has pointer input before a headed select resolves its target, as the pointer commands already do, so a session without it answers at once instead of after the 5 s wait |

## Area L41 — top-level surfaces and snapshot visibility across toolkits

| id | probe | stack | scope | phases.md expectation | observed | verdict | action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L41-1 | Top-level census: every direct child of every application root (role, role name, states, interfaces, name, extents) with the X11 managed clients and viewable override-redirect windows of the same pid, after opening each surface with xdotool; XFCE container (Mousepad Save As, Thunar Create New Folder, Qt main window, bare QWidget, QMessageBox, non-native QFileDialog, top-level QPushButton, Text Editor Open) and the GNOME machine (Text Editor Open and Keyboard Shortcuts, Files New Folder, Calculator Keyboard Shortcuts, Terminal) | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 window enumeration by role `frame`/`dialog`/`window` (L8-2) | Every real top-level window is a root child that implements `Component` and matches an X11 managed client of its pid. Roles seen: 23 `frame` (GTK3, Qt `QMainWindow`), 23 `application` (GTK4), 69 `window`, 16 `dialog` (GTK3 dialog, GTK4 Open File without `MODAL`, Qt `QFileDialog`), 19 `file chooser` (GTK3 Save As: `ACTIVE MODAL SHOWING VISIBLE`, X11 `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE_DIALOG`), 2 `alert` (Qt `QMessageBox`, X11 DIALOG), 20 `filler` (bare `QWidget`), 39 `panel` (GTK4 shortcuts, `MODAL`), 43 `push button` (a Qt `QPushButton` shown as a window). A top-level carries the role of its widget | CONTRADICTS | Window membership is a root child with `Component`; the role is never a gate. A role list cannot be complete: roles 19, 2 and 43 were dropped by the old list |
| L41-2 | Popups against root children, same census with a menu bar menu (Mousepad Alt+F), a GTK3 tooltip (hover on Thunar Home), a GTK4 popover (Text Editor, Calculator, Files F10), a Qt `QMenu.popup` and a GTK3 context menu (Terminal Shift+F10) open | dbus + tool | api-contract | none | The menu bar menu, the GTK3 tooltip (X11 override-redirect 179x53), the GTK4 popovers and the Qt menu (override-redirect 146x44) add no root child. The GTK3 context menu adds a root child role 69 `window`, empty name, `ACTIVE SHOWING VISIBLE`, extents 171x233, one child role 33 `menu` with `FOCUSED SHOWING` and 11 items, and no X11 managed client (only override-redirect); Escape removes it. Chromium menus are blank frames holding a menu bar (L18-7) | NEW-EDGE | A blank, showing top-level whose children are only menus is a popup menu surface: it belongs to menu observation and never to the window list |
| L41-3 | Closed surfaces: the same census after Escape closed each dialog | dbus + tool | app | none | GTK3 and GTK4 remove the root child (file chooser, Open File, New Folder, shortcuts). Qt keeps the closed `QMessageBox` (role 2) and `QFileDialog` (role 16) as root children with only `ENABLED SENSITIVE` (and `RESIZABLE`), extents `[0,0,0,0]`, and no X11 client | NEW-EDGE | A closed husk is told from its states and the missing native window, not from its role; the existing husk filter applies to every role |
| L41-4 | AT-SPI dialog role numbers read from libatspi `Atspi.Role` in the XFCE container | gi-atspi | api-contract | none | `ALERT` 2, `COLOR_CHOOSER` 9, `DIALOG` 16, `FILE_CHOOSER` 19, `FONT_CHOOSER` 22, `FRAME` 23, `PANEL` 39, `TOOL_TIP` 64, `WINDOW` 69, `APPLICATION` 75 | CONFIRMS | The dialog family is these five roles; the number list comes from the library, not from a toolkit |
| L41-5 | Mousepad toolbar in the XFCE container: the ancestor chain of the `Save As...` push button, raw states and extents in screen and window coordinates, against the release CLI `find` and `click` on the same node | dbus + tool | app | §3.4 snapshot states carry the same visibility truth as the action gate | The tool bar (63) has neither `VISIBLE` nor `SHOWING`, extents `[-2147483648,-2147483648,1,1]`; its panel and the button are `VISIBLE` without `SHOWING` at the same sentinel. `find` returned the button with a ref and `"states": []`; headless `click` answered `ACTION_FAILED` "Target is not actionable: visible (live offscreen state is true)". The snapshot projection emits neither `hidden` nor `offscreen`; the live projection derives both | CONTRADICTS | The snapshot must publish `hidden` and `offscreen` from the same rule the action gate uses, without extra reads |
| L41-6 | `launch thunar --activate` in the XFCE container with the session's Thunar daemon running | tool | app | §3.9 launch attaches to an already-running application | The daemon is pid 72, `/proc/72/exe` canonicalises to `/usr/bin/thunar`, its command line is `Thunar --daemon`. The launched `thunar` forwards the request and exits 0; a new Thunar window appears in pid 72; the launch answered `ACTION_FAILED` "'thunar' exited with status 0 without a verified application owner", because the executable identity also compares the argument list | NEW-EDGE | A clean launcher exit with no matching owner is proved by its effect: the one running process of the same executable that shows a new top-level window owns the launch |
| L41-7 | Raw Gio `Properties.GetAll(Accessible)` versus individual `Properties.Get` calls on a disposable Qt widget zoo; repeat a full walk reading optional interfaces only after `GetInterfaces` | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 safe property reads | Ubuntu 24.04.5, PyQt6 6.6.1 binding, `QT_VERSION_STR` and `qVersion()` 6.4.2: `qt_pipeline_probe.py <pid> "Zoo Qt" seq A:Accessible` kills the app with SIGSEGV (139); `P:Accessible.Name` leaves it alive. A guarded single-property walk visits 56 nodes and leaves the app alive; the four identity Gets sent concurrently also leave it alive. Three release CLI snapshots are complete with 38 refs each and raw GetRole still answers afterward. Binding and runtime versions differ; the earlier handoff called this Qt 6.6.1. | CONTRADICTS | Never issue AT-SPI `GetAll`: send Name, Description, ChildCount and AccessibleId as concurrent `Get` calls. Read optional interfaces only when the node advertises them. The transport regression `snapshot_reads_only_named_properties_without_bulk_or_unadvertised_reads` passes with the fix and fails with the original reader (one forbidden bulk read). |
| L41-8 | Raw GTK3 zoo geometry on a private Xvfb 1000x600 display with Openbox versus snapshot state; matched with the VM ref-contract preflight errors | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 snapshot/live visibility parity | Raw expander extents are `[1,602,600,17]`, list `[1,664,600,63]`, and rows start at y=664/685/706. All report `ENABLED`, `SHOWING`, `VISIBLE`, despite lying below the display. Snapshot omitted `offscreen`; the live placement checks display bounds and refused such targets before dispatch. Moving the fixture workflow openers onto the display makes their clicks open raw windows. | CONTRADICTS | Snapshot projection must apply the same display-intersection predicate to its proven screen geometry, with monitor bounds read once per observation. Retained window geometry still governs frame and scroll-container overlap; missing screen/display evidence must not invent offscreen state. |
| L41-9 | Raw semantic routes on private GTK 4.14.5 checkbox/radio, tab-list and list fixtures, with reversible Selection writes | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 headless ref/action parity | Check and both radio widgets are role 7, advertise Accessible/Component/Action, and have empty Action.GetActions. They report CHECKABLE (one radio CHECKED), no Value, and their role-39 parent lacks Selection. TabList and List advertise Selection: SelectChild(1) returns true and independently changes the selected child; SelectChild(0) restores the tabs, ClearSelection restores the list. List index 3 errors for three children; the tab list returns false for out-of-range IsChildSelected instead of an error. | NEW-EDGE | The checkbox/radio bridge exposes no semantic mutation route: do not fabricate disabled state or synthesize input in headless mode. This blocks an unconditional every-ref headless guarantee and needs an owner/toolkit decision. Tabs/lists have a measured route; blanket GTK4 index refusal is too broad, but the list boundary proof does not establish tab index safety. |
| L41-10 | Private Thunar 4.18.8 directory with exactly two known files; raw AT-SPI enumeration across icon/list/icon/list views | dbus + tool | app | §3.4 file-item discoverability | The known filename count is 0 → 2 → 0 → 2. Detailed list view exposes both names as table cells; icon view exposes neither. The probe uses a fresh HOME, Xvfb, D-Bus and owned Thunar process. | NEW-EDGE | Document switching to View as Detailed List (Ctrl+2) and taking a fresh snapshot; an empty icon-view accessibility tree does not prove an empty directory. |
| L41-11 | Raw GTK3 message dialog on a private Xvfb display and session bus | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 alert observation | The visible message dialog exposes role 2 (ALERT), name Information, and ACTIVE/ENABLED/SENSITIVE/SHOWING/VISIBLE, without MODAL. The release alert snapshot completes; clicking its button ref closes the dialog, confirmed through independent raw AT-SPI. | NEW-EDGE | Alert snapshots select SHOWING ALERT nodes, at the window root or nested below it, and resolve their refs with the same predicate. No application identity or version is needed. |
| L41-12 | Private raw GTK4 ListBox with five native rows, one hidden row and a custom header; boundary, index, identity and metadata probes, each repeated twice | dbus | api-contract | §3.4 headless selection safety | Five accessible children can equal five native rows while SelectChild(1) selects hidden row Hidden instead of accessible child Gamma at index 1. IsChildSelected(4) succeeds and (5) errors. A GtkListBoxRow header makes every exposed child role 32 and SELECTABLE, preserving the failure. All rows and header share Parent, interfaces and toolkit-only attributes; no native index or position attribute is exposed. | CONTRADICTS | Do not extend the popup counted-index proof to arbitrary selection containers, even with a selectable-child filter. Accessible identity revalidation does not prove native Selection identity. |
| L41-13 | Repeated private raw Qt list and combo popup option Action calls in two environments, with independent fixture selection and committed-value logs | dbus | api-contract | §3.7 semantic option actions | A selectable, non-checkable LIST_ITEM exposes Toggle. The first call selects it and the second leaves it selected. Popup option calls select rows in both directions but do not change the combo currentText or emit its commit event; the parent's Press and ShowMenu do not commit the highlighted choice either. | CONTRADICTS | Classify this action shape as Select and verify selected state, never checked state. Avoid invoking a selection toggle on an already selected row, refuse unknown state, and do not treat popup highlighting as a committed combo value. |
| L41-14 | Repeated private nested-loop GTK3 dialog.run and Qt exec openers, in container and VM, with independent OPEN/CLOSED fixture events | dbus | api-contract | §3.7 modal delivery | DoAction returns true in under 2 ms in every measured run. Qt exposes the new role-16 dialog after its OPEN event, roughly 110 ms after dispatch. GTK3 blocks application-root GetChildren beyond the 700 ms read timeout while OPEN, and resumes after the scheduled close. | NEW-EDGE | A DoAction reply timeout is not reproduced by these fixtures. Do not add speculative timeout recovery that assumes the application-root observation channel remains readable during a nested dialog. |
| L41-15 | Private GTK3 offscreen horizontal scrollbar with sentinel extents; raw values and fixture events before and after set-value 7 | dbus | api-contract | §3.7 scroll recovery | Before the fix, reveal changes the sibling vertical bar 0→100→200→100 and then refuses. After the fix, two live runs preserve both bar values at 0 and emit no scrollbar change event. Three regression tests fail without the fix and pass with it. | CONTRADICTS | Exclude the target scrollbar's immediate parent from reveal candidates; retain outer scroll ancestors. |
| L41-16 | Preserved Mousepad instance blocked inside an AT-SPI-triggered nested dialog; scoped list-surfaces before and after the shared discovery repair | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 surface uncertainty | Raw application-root reads time out. The earlier scoped surface query returned an empty list; the repaired release returns TIMEOUT with kind frames_unanswered in under one second. A real p2p D-Bus regression distinguishes an unanswered child list from a successfully observed empty list. | CONTRADICTS | Preserve failed frame-read outcomes through scoped discovery; unread surfaces are not absent surfaces. |
| L41-17 | Private GTK3 exported GMenu/GAction Save dialog, raw AT-SPI control, unrelated-focus sentinel, and two complete release CLI Save/Cancel cycles | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.7 nested modal actions | AT-SPI menu activation enters gtk_dialog_run inside the accessibility dispatch source and subsequent reads stall. The process-bound GTK action export opens the same readable dialog when sent without waiting for the modal-lifetime reply. Two independent sentinel runs preserve foreground focus and pointer position. The release CLI resolves the owning X11 window and process, full menu path and exact enabled action signature; Save returns its sheet in surfaces, sheet snapshots complete, and Cancel restores the editor twice. GTK accessible labels include accelerator-padding spaces absent from the exported labels. | NEW-EDGE | Use the existing native semantic export when proven by window metadata and bus-owner PID; normalize mnemonic markers and outer label spacing, reject ambiguity, and never retry an uncertain dispatch. This measured route requires exported GTK metadata on X11. |
| L41-18 | Release menu snapshot and action preflight of the same visible GTK3 Save row, plus inverse projection regression | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 snapshot visibility | A popup row lies outside its small File menu anchor, so clipping to that rectangle falsely reports offscreen while live popup placement accepts it. The regression fails before the repair; two release CLI cycles then report the visible row without offscreen and activate it successfully. | CONTRADICTS | A menu anchor is not a clipping frame for its popup descendants. Retain scroll-container, sentinel and display checks without adding node reads. |
| L41-19 | Private GTK3 application with exported menubar and unnamed context menu; release CLI before/after, followed by two Mousepad Save/Cancel cycles | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.7 menu fallback | Before repair, the complete context snapshot exposes Context Action but clicking refuses because its menu ancestor has no name. After repair, the same ref route reaches Action.DoAction and an independent fixture event file proves activation. Two exported Save/Cancel cycles still open readable sheets and restore the editor; Save takes 534–558 ms. | CONTRADICTS | An available native export does not represent every menu. Unsupported export shapes must fall through before dispatch; unread exports, disabled actions, PID mismatches and uncertain delivery remain fail-closed. |
| L41-20 | Private GTK3 snapshot with a TCP X11 relay forwarding ordinary requests while withholding only RandR replies; release CLI before/after | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.4 optional observation | A completed root snapshot with a 2 s deadline took 2.025 s before repair while RandR GetMonitors never answered. The same snapshot completes in 95 ms after repair, and the relay confirms the stalled RandR request. A healthy full snapshot takes 52 ms. | CONTRADICTS | Monitor decoration is optional: consume its result only if ready after the accessibility walk, otherwise cancel the bounded read without spending the remaining command budget. |
| L41-21 | Private Qt combo and ordinary list, independent application-state file, release CLI reproduction and inverse native action regression | dbus + tool | api-contract | §3.7 selection verification | Selecting the visible Bravo popup option returned delivered_verified while QComboBox.currentText remained Alpha. The regression fails with the original verification and passes when it reads the walked combo owner: the unchanged combo reports ACTION_FAILED with delivered_unverified. Ordinary list selection still verifies Row two then Row one against the independent state file. | CONTRADICTS | A popup highlight does not prove a committed combo value. Carry the nearest combo owner through the existing walked ancestry, restore its window context for read-back, and do not substitute the popup SELECTED bit when that owner's value is unread. |
